### 772. planned jq rewrites re-serialize formatting-sensitive JSON record files
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Add agent-system guidance for in-place edits of formatting-sensitive JSON record files, so a whole-file `jq` rewrite is never authored as the edit mechanism for one.

MEASURED, NOT HYPOTHESIZED. A plan step phrased "with jq, set T4/T6 description ..." was executed against `specs/757_lplus_decidability_successor_task_set/successor-records.json`. Piping that file through `jq` re-serialized it: every compact one-line dependency object -- T1/T2/T3/T5, records the step never intended to touch -- was re-flowed into multi-line form, yielding 44 changed lines for 5 intended field changes. `jq` has no formatting-preserving mode; it parses to a value and prints that value in its own style, so a whole-file pipe through it rewrites the ENTIRE file's layout whether or not the filter touches a given record.

NOTHING IN THE REPOSITORY IS BROKEN. The affected file was repaired in the originating run and is correct as it stands; do not re-edit it, and do not treat this record as a data-repair record. This record's entire deliverable is prose in the source store.

THE ALTERNATIVE MECHANISM IS ALREADY PROVEN AND MUST BE WRITTEN DOWN CONCRETELY, NOT GESTURED AT. The originating run's workaround was: take the HEAD version of the file as the starting bytes; apply the intended field changes as a TEXT-level edit (in that run: two description literals, two `why` values, one appended `_amended` key); separately compute the intended result with `jq`; assert the two agree as JSON under canonicalization -- e.g. `diff <(jq -S . text-edited) <(jq -S . jq-output)` must be empty -- and only then move the text-level edit into place. THE ASSERTION IS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF: it buys back the one thing a text-level edit gives up, namely jq's guarantee that the result is well-formed JSON carrying the intended values. Guidance that says "edit it by hand instead" WITHOUT the equality assertion is worse than no guidance, because a hand edit of JSON silently admits a trailing comma or a broken escape. Write the recipe out as runnable lines a reader can copy, including where the HEAD bytes come from (`git show HEAD:<path>`) and the move-into-place step.

BOTH AUDIENCES MUST BE REACHED, AND THEY READ DIFFERENT FILES. (i) A PLAN AUTHOR must not author "with jq, set X" as a step against such a file in the first place -- the defect originated in a plan step, not in an implementer's improvisation. (ii) An IMPLEMENTER handed such a step must find the alternative without first having to discover the term "re-serialization". A siting that reaches only one of the two leaves half the defect in place; state in the plan which path reaches which audience.

SITING -- DECIDE EXPLICITLY, DO NOT LEAVE IT AMBIGUOUS. Two candidates; the deciding criterion is UNPROMPTED discoverability, i.e. an agent that has just been handed a jq edit step and does not yet know this failure class exists must still land on the guidance. (a) A new section in `context/patterns/jq-escaping-workarounds.md`. That file's title names a DIFFERENT failure class (Claude Code Issue #1132's `!=` escaping), but it is the de facto jq-pitfalls hub: roughly eighteen files across core already point at it, and the deployed `CLAUDE.md`'s "jq Command Safety" section -- sourced from `merge-sources/claudemd.md` -- names it as the full documentation. Discovery is therefore free, at the cost of a file whose title under-describes its contents; if (a) is chosen, widen that file's own framing so a reader is not surprised to meet a second, unrelated failure class inside it. (b) A new sibling pattern file, conceptually cleaner but starting with zero referrers; if (b) is chosen, the referrer wiring and the `index-entries.json` entry are part of THIS change rather than a follow-up, because an unreferenced pattern file is not guidance, it is a file. Either way, add one line to `merge-sources/claudemd.md`'s existing "jq Command Safety" section: that merged section is the surface every session already reads and is the cheapest path to the plan-author audience.

IF SITING (b) IS CHOSEN, WIDEN file_scope BEFORE CREATING THE FILE. The new path must be added through `update-task-status.sh` rather than written into a file_scope-unlisted location silently.

SCOPE DECISION, ALREADY MADE AT FILING TIME: GUIDANCE ONLY, NO HELPER SCRIPT. The originating request asked whether a small formatting-preserving helper script belongs alongside the prose. It does not, and the reasons are recorded here so the question is not re-litigated during research. (1) The proven mechanism is three lines of shell, and the part that is actual WORK -- authoring the text-level edit for this particular file and these particular fields -- cannot be factored into a script; a wrapper would automate only the assertion. (2) A genuinely general formatting-preserving JSON editor (surgical field replacement that preserves per-record compaction and key order) is a substantial program needing its own test suite, deploy registration and ongoing maintenance, out of all proportion to one minor, already-worked-around entry. (3) The nearest existing wrapper, `scripts/state-write.sh`, exists for a reason that does NOT transfer: it serializes many automated, concurrent call sites against one hot file (`specs/state.json`) behind a fail-closed mutex. Record files are edited by a single implementer inside a planned step; a wrapper buys no concurrency safety there and adds an interface to learn. THE REOPEN CONDITION, which the guidance itself should name: if some record file turns out to be edited often enough that the assertion recipe is being retyped repeatedly, revisit the script question then.

WHAT THE GUIDANCE MUST NOT SAY. Do not generalize this into "jq is unsafe for JSON edits". jq remains the correct tool for `specs/state.json` (through `state-write.sh`), for computing the intended value in the recipe above, and for every file whose byte-level layout nobody depends on. State the trigger condition in terms a reader can CHECK before acting: the file is committed with a layout a round-trip through `jq` would not reproduce (hand-maintained compaction, a deliberate key order, mixed compact-and-expanded records), AND the edit is meant to touch a few fields rather than rewrite the file. Name `specs/state.json`'s wrapper as the adjacent-but-different case and say plainly that record files have no such wrapper -- which is exactly why this discipline has to live in prose rather than in a script.

ACCEPTANCE. (1) A reader arriving from the deployed `CLAUDE.md` jq section, or from any existing referrer of the jq pattern file, reaches the new guidance without knowing the term "re-serialization" in advance. (2) The guidance carries a copyable recipe INCLUDING the canonicalized JSON-equality assertion, not merely a prohibition. (3) The guidance states a checkable trigger condition and names at least one concrete file shape that meets it. (4) The guidance states the `state-write.sh` relation and does not imply a wrapper exists for record files. (5) If a new file is created it carries an `index-entries.json` entry and at least one referrer; if an existing file is extended, that file's `index-entries.json` entry (`line_count`, `keywords`) is brought back into agreement with the file. (6) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean` afterwards.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The `.claude/**` path in file_scope is declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; it is NOT the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). The originating file path, the tool name and the field names are durable anchors and may be cited; the originating task number and the issue entry_id may NOT appear in the guidance prose -- they live in this record only.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration of the ladder-frame carrier repair work and approved at the lead level as an agent-system change request. The originating issue-log entry is in `specs/765_ladder_frame_carrier_repair_and_index_deletion/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791760497369_ipMdVR` -- class `tool_side_effect`, severity `minor`, phase `implement`, dispatch_seq 2, resolution `worked_around`. The entry carried NO `suggested_channel` hint, so there is no hint-versus-decision override to state: the orchestrate lead classified it to the agent-system channel on its own judgment, on the ground that the remedy is agent-system guidance rather than repository work product.

RELATED RECORDS, FOR ORIENTATION ONLY -- NO DEPENDENCY EDGES AS FILED. `agent-system/extensions/core/agents/planner-agent.md` is DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED from this record's file_scope even though the plan-author audience makes it a tempting edit target: the plan-format blocked-decision-gate record already claims that path. If research concludes a matching line in `planner-agent.md` is genuinely necessary, add a serializing dependency edge at that point (`update-task-status.sh --depends-on-add`) rather than editing a file another record claims. No other active agent-system record touches the jq pattern file, `index-entries.json`, or `merge-sources/claudemd.md`; re-check that at admission time, since any concurrently filed record that adds a new context file will also touch `index-entries.json`.

### 770. postflight writes detail-free blocking issue entry on partial returns
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Stop the orchestrate postflight from writing a detail-free blocking issue-log entry on every blocker-bearing partial return, and settle that entry's classification.

ROOT CAUSE IS A TYPE MISMATCH, MEASURED NOT HYPOTHESIZED -- THE DETAIL WAS AVAILABLE AND WAS THROWN AWAY. In `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`, the `partial)` arm of the dispatch-status case statement builds the issue narrative with `partial_blockers_detail=$(echo "${handoff:-null}" | jq -r '(.blockers // []) | join("; ")' 2>/dev/null)`, then substitutes a literal fallback string when that comes back empty. The handoff's `blockers[]` is an array of OBJECTS, not strings: `scripts/validate-handoff.sh` requires each entry to carry `phase` and `target` (it counts an entry lacking them as an invalid blocker), and the live returns carry `{phase, target, verbatim_goal, what_was_tried, why_it_failed}`. `jq`'s `join` on an array of objects does not degrade -- it ERRORS (`string ("") and object ... cannot be added`, exit 5). The `2>/dev/null` swallows the error, the command substitution yields the empty string, the `-z` guard fires, and the literal fallback text is what gets recorded. Re-derive this before fixing: run the `join("; ")` expression against a blocker-bearing `.return-meta.json` WITHOUT `2>/dev/null` and read the jq error directly. Note the arm is only reachable when the blocker count is already greater than zero, so the fallback branch as written is reachable ONLY through this error path -- a non-empty array that joins to an empty string is otherwise impossible. That makes the fallback string a permanent output, not a rare degradation.

WHAT THE LOST DETAIL ACTUALLY WAS, AND WHY THE LOSS MATTERS MORE THAN THE NOISE. The discarded `blockers[]` objects carried the refuted goal statement verbatim, what had been tried, and why it failed -- several paragraphs of exactly the content the log exists to preserve. `context/formats/issue-log.md`'s surface-relation table states the MIRROR obligation this call site is implementing: the handoff keeps naming blockers for the immediate successor dispatch, but because `.orchestrator-handoff.json` is overwritten on every dispatch, a `blockers[]` entry about to be written should ALSO be recorded via `issue-record.sh` so the detail survives the overwrite. The call site's own in-code comment cites that obligation by name. The obligation is therefore currently discharged in form and defeated in substance: the entry exists, the detail it was supposed to rescue is gone, and the handoff that held it has since been overwritten. This half of the record is the high-value half and is wholly independent of the classification question below -- land it first.

THE TEST SUITE PASSES OVER THE DEFECT. `scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` already has a regression case for a blocker-bearing partial, and it already uses object-shaped blockers (`target`, `verbatim_goal`, `why_it_failed`). It asserts only the state.json status transition and the aux signal -- never the recorded `what_happened` text -- so the suite is green while the narrative is empty. ACCEPTANCE for the detail half: extend that existing case (do not replace it) to assert that the recorded `issues.jsonl` entry's `what_happened` contains content drawn from the blocker objects, and show the assertion fails against the unmodified script. Decide explicitly how an object is rendered into one prose paragraph -- which subfields are included, in what order, and what happens when an optional subfield is absent -- rather than leaving the format implicit in a jq expression. Removing the `2>/dev/null` on this particular substitution, or otherwise surfacing a jq failure here instead of silently substituting, is in scope: a swallowed error is what converted a fixable bug into an invisible one.

CLASSIFICATION: THREE SEPARABLE QUESTIONS, ONLY ONE OF WHICH THE ORIGINATING REQUEST SETTLED. (1) CLASS. The call site hard-codes `--class "environment"`, whose admission test in `issue-log.md`'s seed-class table is "the local or CI environment (missing binary, version mismatch, permissions) caused the cost". A blocker-bearing partial is almost never that -- in the originating run the blocker was a refuted mathematical statement. The class is wrong for the general case, and it is hard-coded where the actual class is not knowable from the postflight's vantage point; evaluate whether a less specific but honest class, or a class derived from the handoff, is the right remedy. (2) SEVERITY. The call site hard-codes `--severity blocking`. Unlike the class, this appears DEFENSIBLE against the four-rung scale in `issue-log.md`'s "Severity Scale": `blocking` is admitted when "work could not proceed at all ... the dispatch stalled or had to hand off because of it", which is precisely a blocker-bearing partial. Do not reclassify severity merely to reduce channel volume -- if the entry is genuinely blocking, suppressing it by downgrading severity hides a real signal. Argue the severity question on the admission test, and record the argument. (3) VOLUME. The same unresolved blocker is re-recorded on every subsequent partial dispatch: the originating task carries two entries with byte-identical `what_happened`, differing only in `entry_id`, `timestamp`, `dispatch_seq` and `session_id`. Even with full detail restored, N dispatches against one unresolved blocker yield N near-duplicate blocking entries. Consider de-duplication keyed on blocker identity rather than on dispatch.

THE SUPPRESSION IDEA HAS AN ORDERING OBSTACLE -- CHECK IT BEFORE COSTING IT. The originating request asks to consider suppressing the entry entirely when the partial outcome already carries a `user_decision` payload that was relayed and answered. In the current script the `user_decision` relay is a LATER work block (its own section, several hundred lines below the `partial)` arm), so at the moment the issue is written the relay has not yet run. Suppression therefore requires reading `user_decision` off the handoff early, or deferring the issue write until after the relay -- not a one-line guard. Also decide what "answered" means mechanically: `return-metadata-file.md` documents `user_decision` as producer-owned and relayed-never-resolved, so the presence of the payload does not by itself establish that the user answered. If no mechanical test for "answered" exists, say so and scope the suppression to the weaker, checkable condition or drop it with that reason recorded.

DO NOT ENCROACH ON THE CONCLUSION-STAGE FILTER PREDICATE. Whether a reclassified entry still reaches a channel is governed by `context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Filter Predicate" (an entry enters classification only when `resolution` is `open` or `worked_around`). A separate record -- the one covering null-resolution entries being dropped by the conclusion stage -- owns that predicate, `scripts/issue-record.sh`, and `context/formats/issue-log.md`. This record MUST confine its edits to this call site's own arguments and the postflight script plus its test, leaving the predicate, the writer script, and the format document to that record. If that record has already landed a predicate change by the time this one is implemented, read the predicate as it then stands and honor it. If a prose amendment to `issue-log.md` is judged genuinely necessary, add a serializing dependency edge at that point (`update-task-status.sh --depends-on-add`) rather than editing a file another record claims -- the file_scope declared here is deliberately disjoint from it for exactly this reason.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The `.claude/**` path in file_scope is declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; it is NOT the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file is outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Cite durable anchors -- file names, function names, section headings, field names, flag names -- only.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration of the ladder-model cell-stabilization work and approved at the lead level as an agent-system change request. The originating issue-log entries are in `specs/760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/issues.jsonl`, entry_ids `iss_1791711671927_JbPqvi` (dispatch_seq 3) and `iss_1791716590257_0MGDRu` (dispatch_seq 6); both severity `blocking`, class `environment`, resolution `open`. CHANNEL OVERRIDE, RECORDED VISIBLY: both entries' own `suggested_channel` hint reads `follow_up_task`; the orchestrate lead OVERRODE the hint to `agent_system`, on the ground that the defect lies in the orchestrator's own issue-writing path rather than in any task's subject-matter work -- there is no task to follow up, only a writer to fix. `conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Hint-Versus-Decision Rule" sanctions that override provided it is stated visibly, which this paragraph is.

RELATED RECORDS, FOR ORIENTATION ONLY -- NO DEPENDENCY EDGES. Three sibling records from the same orchestration cover the `lake-build-guard.sh` stale-replay defect, the `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` declaration-chunk and Unicode-identifier defects, and the `plan-format.md` blocked-decision-gate and plan-level-Status-ownership provisions; all three touch files disjoint from this one. The null-resolution conclusion-stage record is the nearest neighbour and is the one whose territory this record must not enter, as stated above.

### 769. plan-format: blocked decision gates and plan-level Status ownership
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Add two missing provisions to `plan-format.md`: one admitting a pre-decided `[BLOCKED]` decision-gate phase, and one forbidding a plan from instructing the implementer to write the plan-level `Status` field.

BOTH CONCERNS ARE BATCHED INTO ONE RECORD DELIBERATELY. Each is a short prose provision in the same file, `context/formats/plan-format.md`. Splitting them would create an unordered overlap on one file's prose, which the batching guardrail in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` names as a reason to batch rather than separate. They are otherwise independent and can be landed in either order.

PROVISION 1 -- A `[BLOCKED]` DECISION GATE HAS NO LEGAL PLACE IN THE WAVE GRAPH. `plan-format.md`'s final-phase rule states that a plan's final phase MUST depend, directly or transitively, on every other phase in the plan. A phase that is already `[BLOCKED]` at PLAN time has no consistent position under that rule: making executable phases depend on it DEADLOCKS the implementer, while not depending on it violates the rule's letter. The file's "Decision gates and contingency branches" subsection introduces decision gates but says nothing about one that is blocked before execution begins. Resolved ad hoc in the originating run by placing the blocked gate in wave 1 beside the first executable phase with `Depends on: none`, and noting in the wave table that the final phase re-observes the gate's measurement through the wired probe. PRECEDENT EXISTS AND SHOULD BE READ BEFORE DRAFTING: `specs/archive/006` plan 05 and `specs/archive/008` plan 03 (locate the exact paths by globbing the archive -- the directory names carry slugs). A one-paragraph provision in the "Decision gates and contingency branches" subsection settles it. The provision must say explicitly how such a gate relates to the final-phase transitive-dependency rule, so that a plan validator and a human reader reach the same verdict on a plan containing one; a provision that merely permits the shape without reconciling it against the final-phase rule leaves the ambiguity in place.

PROVISION 2 -- PLANNER-SIDE GUIDANCE PERMITS AUTHORING AN INSTRUCTION THE IMPLEMENTER IS FORBIDDEN TO EXECUTE. In the originating run a plan's final phase instructed the implementer to set the plan-level `- **Status**:` field to `[BLOCKED]`. The `lean-implementation-agent` contract (its MUST-NOT item citing `context/contracts/plan-status-ownership.md`) reserves that field for `update-plan-status.sh` via `update-task-status.sh` postflight. The agent CORRECTLY followed the contract and annotated the checklist item as a skipped deviation -- so nothing malfunctioned at run time; the defect is that the instruction was writable at all. THE FIX BELONGS ON THE PLANNER/PLAN-FORMAT SIDE so such an instruction is never authored. Concretely: `plan-format.md` already documents the plan-level `- **Status**:` field, its required-field list, and its trailing-annotation rule under "Plan-level vs. phase-level markers" -- add, at that same place, an explicit statement that the field is owned by `update-plan-status.sh` and that NO phase checklist item may instruct a writer to set it, together with what a planner should write instead when it wants a plan to end up `[BLOCKED]`. That last clause is load-bearing: a prohibition with no sanctioned alternative will be worked around. Evaluate whether `agents/planner-agent.md` needs a matching line, since that file carries the plan template and the required-field checks the planner actually executes; if `plan-format.md` alone is judged sufficient, say so in the plan and drop the planner-agent path from the edit set rather than leaving it ambiguous.

ACCEPTANCE. (1) A reader of `plan-format.md` alone can determine whether a plan containing a pre-decided `[BLOCKED]` gate conforms, and can predict where that gate sits in the wave table. (2) A reader of `plan-format.md` alone can determine that the plan-level `Status` field is not writable from a phase checklist item, and can find the sanctioned alternative. (3) The new prose does not contradict `context/contracts/plan-status-ownership.md` or `context/standards/status-markers.md` -- in particular, status-markers.md's distinction between a target argument passed to `update-task-status.sh` and the RESTING state that actually persists must be left intact. (4) No change to the closed six-value phase-marker enum or to the `### Phase N: {name} [STATUS]` heading contract, both of which have mechanical consumers (`update-phase-status.sh`, `update-plan-status.sh`, `update-task-status.sh --phase-check`) that this record must not disturb.

### 768. lean-challenge-snapshot swallows declarations and mishandles Unicode names
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Fix two independent defects in `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` that both cause declarations to be silently lost or mangled during snapshot assembly.

DEFECT (a) -- DECLARATION-CHUNK BOUNDARY OVER-REACH SWALLOWS `abbrev` AND ANONYMOUS `instance`. The script forces each theorem's chunk body to `:= sorry` running up to the NEXT MATCHED declaration, where the match is the alternation `(theorem|lemma|def|instance)` followed by a REQUIRED identifier. An `abbrev`, or an anonymous `instance`, sitting between two theorems therefore matches nothing and is silently absorbed into the preceding declaration's sorry body. The script's own comment concedes the mechanism in as many words: it forces the "body to `sorry` regardless of what the block actually contains." The alternation appears at more than one site (near the goal-name extractor, the body-forcing assembler, and the git-pinned signature reader) -- enumerate every occurrence rather than patching the first one found, since a fix applied to only one site leaves the others disagreeing about what counts as a declaration.

DEFECT (b) -- ASCII-ONLY IDENTIFIER REGEX. The identifier pattern `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-zA-Z0-9_']*` appears at the same three sites. Lean identifiers routinely contain non-ASCII characters, and such a name either escapes the Goals cross-check entirely or is TRUNCATED at its first non-ASCII character -- which is worse than escaping, because a truncated name can collide with a different real declaration. Note in passing that the character class as written contains a redundant range (`A-Za-zA-Z0-9`); that is harmless today but is a sign the pattern has not been audited, and all three copies should be replaced by one shared definition rather than three independently edited literals.

OBSERVED WORKAROUNDS, WHICH ARE THE EVIDENCE THAT THE DEFECTS BITE IN PRACTICE. In the originating run: every supporting definition was hoisted ahead of the first theorem and rewritten as an `abbrev`; four supporting facts were made sorry-bodied `abbrev`s rather than Goals; and the refutation probe renamed its Unicode identifiers to ASCII. None of these were desired for their own sake -- each exists solely to route around one of the two defects above, which means the script is currently shaping the Lean source that callers are allowed to write.

SUGGESTED REMEDIES TO EVALUATE (from the originating request; evaluate, do not assume). (1) A `--dry-run` mode that also COMPILES the assembled module. Note `--dry-run` ALREADY EXISTS -- its documented behavior is to print the assembled module and the resolved `theorem_names` to stdout -- so the ask is to EXTEND it with a compile step, not to add the flag. This is the higher-leverage of the two remedies because it converts silent swallowing into a loud compile error, catching defect (a) and any future variant of it without the assembler having to enumerate every declaration keyword Lean will ever have. Decide whether the compile runs by default under `--dry-run` or behind a further opt-in, and whether a compile failure is fatal or advisory. (2) A Unicode-aware identifier regex, for defect (b). Consider whether the Python sites can use a character class keyed on Lean's actual identifier grammar rather than a hand-rolled approximation.

ACCEPTANCE -- EXTEND `tests/test-lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`, WHICH ALREADY EXISTS, PLUS ITS `fixtures/` DIRECTORY. Add failing-first cases for: an `abbrev` between two theorems; an anonymous `instance` between two theorems; and a declaration with a non-ASCII name appearing in both the Goals cross-check and the assembled module. Demonstrate each fails against the unmodified script. If the compile step is added to `--dry-run`, cover the case where the assembled module does not compile and assert the chosen fatal-or-advisory behavior.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`lean`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/lean`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The `.claude/**` paths in file_scope are declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are NOT the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file is outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Cite durable anchors -- file names, function names, section headings, flag names -- only.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration of the ladder-model cell-stabilization work and approved at the lead level as an agent-system change request. The originating issue-log entry is in `specs/760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791710205702_yQf0sD`, severity minor, class "tooling bug or gap", resolution worked_around, hinted channel `agent_system` (hint not overridden). The two defects are batched into one record because they live in one script and share all three regex sites -- splitting them would create an unordered overlap on the same file.

### 767. lake-build-guard replays stale result for env lean FILE
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Stop `lake-build-guard.sh` from replaying a stale shared result when the verified unit is a single file passed through to `env lean`.

OBSERVED LIVE, NOT HYPOTHESIZED. `bash .claude/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh build -- env lean FILE` REPLAYED the previous invocation's captured output -- errors citing line numbers of the OLD contents of FILE -- after FILE had been rewritten at the same path. The run was worked around with `--no-share`. The staleness fingerprint evidently does not cover an arbitrary file argument forwarded to `env lean`.

WHY THIS IS THE HIGHEST-VALUE ITEM IN ITS BATCH. A guard that silently replays a stale result undermines EVERY single-file verification in the system, including verifications later cited as evidence in reports, summaries and gate decisions. A stale green replay is indistinguishable from a fresh green run to the caller unless the caller inspects stderr for the `lake-build-guard: REPLAY:` marker -- the script's own usage text says exactly this under `--no-share`. The failure mode is silent acceptance of an obsolete result, not a crash, so nothing downstream flags it.

WHERE TO LOOK -- RE-DERIVE RATHER THAN TRUST; the following is a snapshot of a ~1242-line script. `collect_fingerprint_files()` documents itself as listing every `*.lean` source under the project tree. `compute_fingerprint()` defaults to `stat` mode (file METADATA), with content hashing available only opt-in via `LAKE_BUILD_GUARD_FINGERPRINT=hash`; the header notes hash mode exists specifically to close a gap stat mode leaves open. `compute_scope_key()` keys sharing on scope, and `decide_sharing()` gates the replay. The header's own list of known limitations ALREADY concedes that a caller "cannot distinguish a replay from a fresh run without passing --no-share". `LAKE_SUBCOMMANDS` includes `env` and `lean`, so an `env lean FILE` invocation passes the subcommand allowlist unremarked.

DISCRIMINATE BETWEEN TWO CANDIDATE MECHANISMS BEFORE FIXING ANYTHING -- they have different remedies and only measurement settles which occurred. (i) The rewritten FILE is not a member of the fingerprinted set at all -- it lies outside the collected root, or outside the `*.lean` glob -- so rewriting it CANNOT move the fingerprint, and no amount of strengthening the hash helps. (ii) FILE is in the set, but stat-mode metadata did not change discernibly between the two runs, in which case content hashing for named file arguments is sufficient. Reproduce the replay mechanically first; do not infer the mechanism from the header's prose.

DECIDE AND LAND ONE OF TWO. The originating request names both and they are NOT equivalent. (a) Extend the fingerprint and/or the scope key to cover the explicit file arguments of the forwarded command, so an `env lean FILE` invocation's shareability tracks FILE's own content. (b) Refuse to share a result for an `env lean ...` invocation at all -- treat it as implicitly `--no-share`. Option (b) is strictly safer and much cheaper to reason about, at the cost of giving up sharing for a real use case. Option (a) preserves sharing but must be shown correct for a file lying OUTSIDE the fingerprinted tree, which is the harder case and the one mechanism (i) above describes. If (a) is chosen, prefer content hashing over stat metadata for explicitly named file arguments regardless of the ambient `LAKE_BUILD_GUARD_FINGERPRINT` mode: stat-mode equality is the weaker predicate and this is precisely where it failed.

ACCEPTANCE -- A FAILING-FIRST REGRESSION TEST IN `tests/test-lake-build-guard.sh`, WHICH ALREADY EXISTS AND MUST BE EXTENDED RATHER THAN REPLACED. The test must: (1) run `build -- env lean FILE`; (2) rewrite FILE at the SAME path so its compile outcome changes; (3) re-run the identical command WITHOUT `--no-share`; (4) assert the second run reports the NEW outcome. Demonstrate the test fails against the unmodified script before landing the fix. Additionally, the script's documented STALENESS POLICY -- the numbered conditions in its header, which include "the record's POST-build fingerprint equals the WAITER's CURRENT fingerprint" and "a missing or empty recorded scope_key is treated as NOT shareable (fail closed)" -- MUST end up agreeing in writing with whatever behavior is landed. The acceptance condition for the documentation half is that a reader of the header alone can predict the file-argument case.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The `.claude/**` paths in file_scope are declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are NOT the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file is outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Cite durable anchors -- file names, function names, section headings, flag names -- only.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration of the ladder-model cell-stabilization work and approved at the lead level as an agent-system change request. The originating issue-log entry is in `specs/760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791710205724_8rTs68` (the change request also referenced it as `iss_1791711671927_8rTs68`), severity minor, class "tooling bug or gap", resolution worked_around, hinted channel `agent_system` (hint not overridden).

### 765. Ladder frame carrier repair and index deletion
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 760
- **Parent**: Task 760
- **Plan**: [765_ladder_frame_carrier_repair_and_index_deletion/plans/01_ladder-carrier-repair-index-deletion.md]
- **Summary**: [765_ladder_frame_carrier_repair_and_index_deletion/summaries/01_ladder-carrier-repair-index-deletion-summary.md]

**Description**: Repair the ladderFrame carrier defect and the refuted deadline index in the successor records. Two findings landed as the twelfth wired evidence probe (specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/ladder-cell-refutation.lean): (F1) ladderFrame's fwd/bwd seriality hypotheses quantify over the full carrier Q x N x N while Rlad requires an idle state to carry c = 0, so the hypotheses are unsatisfiable for every object and the frame has no instances -- Adm must be a carrier restriction, not a side condition; (F2) cell stabilization over the counter pair (c, n) is refuted for every bound, because the gap n - c stays observable through a closure of size 12 while each counter is arbitrarily large. Scope: the general salvage block already exists machine-checked and fully general over {Q} [Finite Q] [Nonempty Q] inside that probe (no_fwd, no_bwd_of_idle, Adm, LadderState and its Nonempty instance, RladAdm, RladAdm_fwd, RladAdm_bwd, ladderFrameAdm, ladderFrameAdm_isRegular, ladderModel), depends only on RunLayer.lean and ofSlicedStepFinBranch*, and carries zero new proof obligations -- the transplant is a copy plus wiring. Fix the filed statement's type shape en route: a sliced frame's world state is Z x W, so cellAt ... (q,c,n) z does not typecheck. GUARDRAILS: keep the fixture-bound statements (cell_ne, exhibited_form_refuted, exists_form_refuted and their Q = Bool support) in the evidence probe, landing only the general F1 negatives in the library, so no witness fixture enters a library module; leave ladderFrame in RunLayer.lean instance-free and C14-pinned, adding ladderFrameAdm alongside rather than repairing in place. The refuted index Q x Fin (B+1) x Fin (B+1) has NO proved replacement anywhere -- the candidate (q, min c B2, min n B2, clip (n - c)) is research-level and unproved -- so the dependent records must have the refuted index claim DELETED and recorded as an open sub-problem, never re-stated over a concrete substitute, which would file an unproved claim. The cell set itself is untouched: anchorCells_finite and anchorCells_ncard_le still stand, so what was lost is the computable index of which cell a ladder state has, not the finiteness of the set of cells. No upper bound, no complexity claim; every quantity is a count. Durable anchors only outside specs/**.

### 764. Specs only gate and task reference lint debt
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 757

**Description**: Close the specs/**-only phase verification gap and the repo-wide task-reference lint debt

WHY THIS EXISTS. Two verification findings recorded during the L+ successor-task-set round, both worked around rather than fixed, and both about what a gate actually attests to rather than about any mathematics.

(1) THE SPECS-ONLY COVERING CHECK ATTESTS TO NOTHING. `verify-deploy.sh --only-gate 3,4` was used as the covering check for a newly written `specs/**` document. In a deploy-consumer tree both gates SKIP — doc-lint and the task-reference lint apply only to the source store — so the invocation exits 0 having run 0 checks. An exit-0 that verified nothing is worse than no check at all, because a plan item can cite it as satisfied. Decide what a phase whose entire `Files to modify` set lies under `specs/**` should actually attest to, and make the chosen gate either run real checks in a consumer tree or report explicitly that it is inapplicable here (a distinguishable exit or a loud SKIP summary), rather than silently exiting 0 with a zero check count. Re-derive the current SKIP behaviour from `verify-deploy.sh` before acting; this description reflects a reading from 2026-10-10.

(2) THE REPO-WIDE TASK-REFERENCE LINT CANNOT REACH 0. `check-task-references.sh` exits 1 on roughly 198 pre-existing occurrences outside `specs/`, concentrated in `Boneyard/**` READMEs and similar, none of them in a file the originating round touched. The count is a standing-debt signal, not a regression signal, so any plan attestation naming "`check-task-references.sh` exits 0" as a criterion is unsatisfiable today and gets annotated as altered every time. Re-count first, then choose one disposition and carry it through: clean the occurrences, extend the exemption taxonomy in `context/standards/task-reference-exemptions.md` to cover the classes that are legitimately exempt (`Boneyard/**` is a plausible candidate — argue it, do not assume it), or give the script a baseline mode so a new occurrence is distinguishable from the standing set. The `task-ref-ok` marker convention and `scripts/lib/task-reference-patterns.sh` are the mechanical source of truth; any exemption decision lands there and in the standards file, never as a one-off suppression at a call site.

SCOPE. Both halves are changes to the agent system in the source store resolved via `.claude-extensions.json`, never hand-authored under `.claude/**`. Each half may be done independently; sequence them in whatever order the plan prefers.

NON-GOALS. No change to any gate's pass criteria beyond what (1) and (2) name. No mass rewrite of `Boneyard/**` content itself — if cleaning is the chosen disposition for (2), it is a mechanical task-number-reference edit, not a content revision. No new lint.

### 763. Ladder lift decidability corollary
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 761, Task 762

**Description**: AMENDED 2026-10-11 (index deletion): the finite cell index this record assembled its object from is refuted and deleted below; see specs/765_ladder_frame_carrier_repair_and_index_deletion/plans/01_ladder-carrier-repair-index-deletion.md.

STATEMENT (library, research-first; new module `RaySummary/Lift.lean`). The (W) row's lift from one seam to validity — "one presentation-level object witnesses ¬ PlusValidZTime φ for some regular ℤ-model, history and time" — and obligation (3)'s second half ("every seam but the one"):
  `theorem not_plusValidZTime_iff_ladderRefutes (φ : PlusFormula) : ¬ PlusValidZTime φ ↔ ∃ (Q : Type) (_ : Fintype Q) (_ : DecidableEq Q) (lab) (step) (pend) (fwd : ∀ t x, ∃ y, RladAdm step pend t x y) (bwd : ∀ t x, ∃ y, RladAdm step pend (t - 1) y x), Fintype.card Q ≤ objBound φ ∧ LadderCons φ lab step pend ∧ LadderRefutes φ lab step pend` (`Adm` is a CARRIER restriction defined in `RaySummary/LadderModel.lean` — `LadderState Q pend := {x : Q × ℕ × ℕ // x.2.1 ∈ Adm pend x.1}` — not an object-side hypothesis; the object ranges over the admissible carrier, with its seriality `fwd`/`bwd` stated over `RladAdm` on that carrier)
(→), completeness: a refuting regular ℤ-model has cells bounded by `anchorCells_ncard_le`, per-cell presentations by (C)'s general row (`exists_hitPresented`) — and the step from finitely many CELLS to finitely many ladder STATES per cell has NO support: `ladderCell_stabilizes` is REFUTED for every bound (`specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/ladder-cell-refutation.lean`: `cell_ne`, `exhibited_form_refuted`, `exists_form_refuted`; the gap n − c is observable) and is DELETED from this assembly. How a refuting model's finitely many cells (`anchorCells_finite`, `anchorCells_ncard_le`, untouched) are assembled into ONE finite ladder object — a computable index of WHICH cell a ladder state has — is an OPEN SUB-PROBLEM of this record: recorded here, not solved, and never re-stated over a concrete substitute (the candidate carrying a clipped gap coordinate is research-level and unproved). (←), soundness: `ladderRealized` at the refuting cell gives a regular ℤ-model (`ladderFrameAdm_isRegular`, the frame on the admissible carrier; `ladderFrame` has no instances, `no_fwd`/`no_bwd_of_idle`), a history and a time where φ fails. Depth k = 1 throughout.

THE COROLLARY — THE AIM — IS STATED HERE AND NOWHERE EARLIER: `Decidable (PlusValidZTime φ)` by bounded enumeration over objects of size ≤ objBound φ (finitely many on `Fin n`; `LadderCons`/`LadderRefutes` decidable on a Fintype, as `decidableAnchorConsFin` already is on a concrete finite object). `Decidable (PlusValidZTime` has zero statement-level occurrences in the tree today (14 hits, all prose denials); no predecessor record may state it.

STATUS. Not costable until both predecessors have landed shapes and until the open index sub-problem above is resolved; the research phase re-derives the statement from `LadderRealize.lean` and `HitComplete.lean` as landed. Honest outcomes: landed; or [BLOCKED] on whichever predecessor did not close or on the open sub-problem — never sorry, axiom, vacuous definition, or a decidability claim from the semi-decidable ∃-object form without the bound (the README: without the bound the composition theorem is "at best semi-decidable — and NOT Decidable (PlusValidZTime φ)").

GUARDRAILS: (a) the ladder object is a time-axis object over the closure alphabet; (b) its slice is infinite — the object bounds cells and deadline classes, never the fibre, and no finite-fibre assumption may be introduced to make the enumeration finite (the bound comes from counts); (c) `objBound φ` is a count (its shape is part of the open sub-problem; no figure exists) and the corollary is a `Decidable` instance, not a complexity statement — the held 2EXPTIME ceiling is neither cited nor approached; (d) zero debt: the enumeration is the tree's own, no external decidability theorem, no cslib require; (e) if T4/T5 land forward-only the lift is forward-only and says so — nothing beyond `Probe719Backward.pastStab_iff_allBwdPathsMeet` is claimed about ⊡(Pp). Dispatch with --lit (HWZ 2000 / GKWZ 2003 indexed, orientation only).

FILE SCOPE: new `Lift.lean` plus the shared library set. Durable anchors only outside specs/**. Cross-set collisions left to the admission gate: 744, 410. Logical edges on T4 and T5.

NOT COSTABLE, and why: statement fixed by T4/T5. Design of record: `specs/757_lplus_decidability_successor_task_set/followup-scope-spec.md` Sections C, D and H6.

**Description**: STATEMENT (library, research-first; new module `RaySummary/HitComplete.lean`). Requirement (C)'s general row, verbatim from the RaySummary README's verdict table and "(W) … obligation (ii)" item 2 — run-layer phase 3, the completeness half (model → object): for every regular ℤ-model M, seam (τ, t), formula φ and depth 1 ≤ k, a finite labelled presentation with a computable bound in φ:
  `theorem exists_hitPresented (M : TaskModel F.toTaskFrame) [F.IsRegular] (τ) (t) (φ) (k) (hk : 1 ≤ k) : ∃ (Q : Type) (_ : Fintype Q) (_ : DecidableEq Q) (lab : Q → (clSig φ).preds → Bool) (step : Q → Q → Bool), Fintype.card Q ≤ sizeBound φ k ∧ HitPresented M τ t φ lab step k`
with `sizeBound φ k` a COUNT exhibited in the statement (the fixture's was `Nat.card (KType (clSig Fp) k) + 6`, `card_QA_presentationSize`), never an ∃ L and never a complexity bound. `HitPresented` is `RaySummary/Trace.lean`'s Hit-level hypothesis; the trace-level `Presented` is refuted as a completeness carrier (`Probe735.not_presented`) and is not the statement.

STATUS: OPEN MATHEMATICS. One positive fixture (`Probe745.hitPresented_modelA`, box/stab-free, finite-width slice), no Hit-level negative anywhere, no general argument. The research phase of this record comes FIRST and must re-derive the statement against the tree, including T2's verdict: if T2 returned a Hit-level negative this statement is false and the record is a Stage 0 user matter — do not re-scope or abandon it as an agent. Ingredients that exist: `kTypeOf_listSeg_replicate_eventually_periodic_bounded` (Compose.lean), `inFamily_of_shrink_run` (Transport.lean; shrink direction, single segment); the unconditioned transport ↔ (threshold 3·(m₀+p)) is a recorded non-goal of the bound-half record and may be needed here. HWZ 2000 Def. 12/Thm 14 and GKWZ 2003 Thm 13.6 are indexed (`specs/literature-index.json`) as the literature description of the quasimodel-realisation step; they orient the shape and source no claim — dispatch with --lit.

CONTINGENCY. If the general argument does not close, the honest outcome is [BLOCKED] with the obstruction recorded; never sorry, axiom, vacuous definition, an external Büchi/MSO decidability theorem cited in place of a proof, or a finite-width/finite-fibre hypothesis smuggled into M.

GUARDRAILS: (a) `HitPresented` is over the closure alphabet `clSig φ`; (b) M ranges over ALL regular ℤ-models — no width or fibre assumption; (c) `sizeBound` is a count, no running-time claim, the held 2EXPTIME ceiling rules proposals out only; (d) zero debt, no cslib require; (e) if the landed form is forward-only (as every (C)/(S) fixture is), say so; nothing bears on ⊡(Pp).

### 761. Ladder realization theorem
- **Status**: [RESEARCHED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 760
- **Research**: [761_ladder_realization_theorem/reports/01_ladder-realization-theorem.md]

**Description**: AMENDED 2026-10-11 (carrier repair): the frame this record is stated on is the admissible-carrier frame of RaySummary/LadderModel.lean; see specs/765_ladder_frame_carrier_repair_and_index_deletion/plans/01_ladder-carrier-repair-index-deletion.md.

STATEMENT (library; new module `RaySummary/LadderRealize.lean`). Run-layer phase 2's soundness half (object → model), the ladder analogue of `Realize.lean`'s `Cons`/`cons_localRun`/`presented_modelOf`, stated at the HIT level per ladder cell:
  `structure LadderCons (φ : PlusFormula) (lab) (step) (pend) : Prop`  -- `Cons`'s fields with the liveness fields `ulive`/`slive` replaced by the ladder's structural liveness (c strictly decreases while pending, by `Count`), and with the box/stab letters PINNED per cell by the anchor layer's equations: `lab q (box ψ)` is one global bit (`box_mem_tpOf_iff`), the stab letter at `(q, c, n)` is that cell's bit (`stab_mem_tpOf_iff`); `anchorCons_anchorCells` is the completeness side of exactly those equations
  `theorem ladderCons_localRun …`  -- every ladder run satisfies the run conditions
  `theorem ladderRealized … (k : ℕ) (hk : 1 ≤ k) : HitCl (ladderModel …) τ t φ k = ↑(hitOf (truncate B …) … (clNow φ) k)`  -- per ladder cell, for ALL φ, box/stab letters INCLUDED, the cell being `cellAt (ladderModel …) φ (t, ⟨(q, c, n), h⟩)` on the world state `ℤ × LadderState Q pend` (the `ℤ` component is invisible to `cellAt`, `cellAt_eq_cellAt`, but must be written). NO finite index of which cell a ladder state has is provided by any predecessor: the index `Q × Fin (B+1) × Fin (B+1)` is refuted (`ladder-cell-refutation.lean`: `cell_ne`, `exhibited_form_refuted`, `exists_form_refuted`) and is DELETED here; which cell a ladder state has is an OPEN SUB-PROBLEM, recorded and not re-stated over any substitute. The SET of cells stays finite and bounded (`anchorCells_finite`, `anchorCells_ncard_le`)
The box/stab clause is the acceptance criterion: it is where obligation (3)'s first half ("(S) beyond its landed corner": box/stab-bearing φ) lands, by the README's own assignment of that limitation to (W). No standalone (S) record exists for it (agent decision D1, re-openable by /revise).

WHY HIT-LEVEL, WHY THIS FRAME. Two machine-checked facts (wired probe `w-soundness-shape-refuted.lean`): WIDTH — `Probe747.modelOf_incomplete`: `modelOf` on any `Cons` presentation validates `Φ.neg` while `¬ PlusValidZTime Φ.neg`, whatever clauses are added, because `ofSlicedStep` on a Fintype has finite width; COLLAPSE — `Probe747.stab_iff_of_presented_nowUnique`: at a trace-level `Presented` seam with `nowUnique`, ψ ↔ ⊡ψ for every closure formula, so trace-level realization cannot witness any ⊡-refutation. Hence the hypothesis is `HitPresented`-shaped per cell and the frame is `ladderFrameAdm` on the admissible carrier `LadderState Q pend := {x : Q × ℕ × ℕ // x.2.1 ∈ Adm pend x.1}` (`RaySummary/LadderModel.lean`; slice infinite — `(q, 0, n)` is admissible for every `n`, `Rlad_slice_infinite` one level down; regular by `ladderFrameAdm_isRegular`), with the model `ladderModel` reading the object component; `ladderFrame` itself has no instances (`no_fwd`, `no_bwd_of_idle`) and stays beside it, untouched. `presented_modelOf` is untouched and not re-proved.

DEPTH. `1 ≤ k` stated; the consumer depth is k = 1 (every landed bridge carries `1 ≤ k`; the closure alphabet makes nesting visible through letters).

CONTINGENCY. If the theorem does not close, mark [BLOCKED] with the goal state recorded; never sorry, axiom, vacuous definition, a trace-level hypothesis, or any finite-fibre/finite-width assumption. T3/T4 keep standalone value whatever T2 reports (the HWZ 2000 Theorem 14 ⇐ direction for this semantics — a literature description that sources no claim).

GUARDRAILS: (a) closure-indexed letters over the time axis, box/stab bits included; (b) infinite width, no fibre bound; (c) no upper bound — B and the presentation size are counts; (d) zero debt: no Büchi/MSO lemma cited in place of a proof, no cslib require; (e) if the landed form is forward-only, say so; no device-discrimination claim.

FILE SCOPE: new `LadderRealize.lean` plus the shared library set (`RaySummary.lean`, `FormalSystem.lean`, `KTypeSummaryAxiomTest.lean`, `check-module-invariants.sh`, `docs/theorem-index.md`, RaySummary README, `typst/generated/status.typ`). Durable anchors only outside specs/**. Cross-set collisions left to the admission gate: 744 (README, invariants), 410 (status.typ). Logical edge on T3.

### 760. Ladder model cell stabilization lemma
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 758, Task 759
- **Research**: [760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/reports/01_ladder-cell-stabilization-refuted.md]
- **Plan**: [760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/plans/02_land-salvaged-ladder-model.md]
- **Summary**: [760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/summaries/02_land-salvaged-ladder-model-summary.md]

**Description**: STATEMENT (library, over a general finite object; new module `RaySummary/LadderModel.lean`). First a MODEL on the landed frame, since `ladderFrame` (`RaySummary/RunLayer.lean`) is a `FrameOver intOrder` and `cellAt` needs a `TaskModel`:
  `def ladderModel (step : Q → Q → Bool) (pend : Q → Bool) (fwd) (bwd) (lab : Q → Atom → Bool) : TaskModel (ladderFrame step pend fwd bwd).toTaskFrame`  -- valuation reads the object component q
then the admissibility set `Adm` (named in the RaySummary README as "c ∈ Adm q … named here and not costed", defined nowhere — this record defines it: which objects supply `ladderFrame`'s `fwd`/`bwd`; a pending state at `c = 0` needs a discharging successor), and the lemma, over BOTH ladder counters:
  `theorem ladderCell_stabilizes (φ : PlusFormula) … : ∀ (q : Q) (c c' n n' : ℕ) (z : ℤ), (plusSubformulaClosure φ).card ≤ c → (plusSubformulaClosure φ).card ≤ c' → (plusSubformulaClosure φ).card ≤ n → (plusSubformulaClosure φ).card ≤ n' → cellAt (ladderModel …) φ (q, c, n) z = cellAt (ladderModel …) φ (q, c', n') z`
with the bound `B := (plusSubformulaClosure φ).card` EXHIBITED in the statement; fallback `∃ B : ℕ, ∀ q c c' n n' z, B ≤ c → … → cellAt … = cellAt …` if the exhibited bound fails. Either form yields finitely many deadline classes per counter and a finite cell index `Q × Fin (B+1) × Fin (B+1)`, which the realization record builds on.

WHY BOTH COUNTERS. `Count` (`RunLayer.lean`): while pending `c' + 1 = c` (the region ends in exactly c steps — a letter needing d more steps is absent at c < d); at an idle state `c' = n` (the next region's length is n — a letter looking d steps into it depends on n for n < d). The README's "in the deadline counter c" is a shorthand; its "cells indexed by (q, deadline class)" is correct read as the class of the pair. Agent decision (D2/D3 of the design record), re-openable by /revise: this lemma is its own record, split from the realization theorem at the costability boundary the README draws ("no cost figure until that lemma is stated"); fold back with one /revise if preferred.

B IS A COUNT. The sentence the tree attaches to `anchorCells_ncard_le` applies verbatim: a cardinality bound on cells, syntactic in |cl φ|, not a complexity bound of any kind. Depth: none (cells are closure-type sets); the run layer's consumer depth is k = 1.

CONTINGENCY. If T1 refuted stabilization at the fixture, the ∃ B form is false too: mark [BLOCKED] with the obstruction in the plan file. Never sorry, axiom, vacuous definition, trace-level (`Presented`) hypothesis, or a capped ladder (`Probe750.capped_carrier_finite` shows that carrier finite — the shape `not_finite_width_fmp` refutes).

GUARDRAILS: (a) time-axis object over the closure alphabet; (b) the slice `Q × ℕ × ℕ` is infinite (`Rlad_slice_infinite`) — the lemma bounds the INDEX of cells, never the fibre; (c) no upper bound; (d) zero debt, no cslib require; (e) direction-neutral statement, but every probe it is compared with is forward-only — say so.

FILE SCOPE (shared library set). New `LadderModel.lean`; aggregator `RaySummary.lean` and `FormalSystem.lean`; C14 pins in `KTypeSummaryAxiomTest.lean` and `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh`; `docs/theorem-index.md` rows (count-not-complexity sentence); the RaySummary README; `typst/generated/status.typ` (regenerated by the pre-commit hook). Durable anchors only outside specs/** — no task numbers. Cross-set collisions left to the admission gate: 744 (README, invariants script), 410 (status.typ). Logical edge on T1; serialization edge on T2 (README).

NOT COSTABLE, and why: the bound shape and `Adm` are fixed by T1's verdict; no comparable statement exists in the tree (the anchor layer, 418 lines, is a different kind of statement). Design of record: `specs/757_lplus_decidability_successor_task_set/followup-scope-spec.md` Section A and H3.

### 759. Node hit presented probe
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 758
- **Research**: [759_node_hit_presented_probe/reports/01_node-hit-presented-probe.md]

- **Summary**: [759_node_hit_presented_probe/summaries/01_node-hit-presented-probe-summary.md]

**Description**: STATEMENT. A wired probe on the LIBRARY fixture `NoFiniteWidth.M` at a seam `(t, Node.pre k)` with `τ` the canonical history through it (`canon`, `path_eq_canon`), for a ⊡-bearing formula `Φ'` := the fixture witness `Φ`, fallback `D`, then `⊡Xp` (recorded in that order in the header; the chosen formula is written into the statement), at every depth `1 ≤ k`. EITHER headline is the deliverable, and the probe records which:
  `theorem hitPresented_node (t : ℤ) (k : ℕ) (hk : 1 ≤ k) : HitPresented NoFiniteWidth.M τ t Φ' (labN L) (stepN L) k`  -- positive: a presentation of size L, exhibited as a COUNT in the statement (as `presentationSize k = Nat.card (KType (clSig Fp) k)` is in `Probe745.hitPresented_modelA`), never ∃ L
  `theorem not_hitPresented_node (t : ℤ) (k : ℕ) (hk : 1 ≤ k) {Q} [Fintype Q] [DecidableEq Q] (lab) (step) : ¬ HitPresented NoFiniteWidth.M τ t Φ' lab step k`  -- negative: (C)'s general row is FALSE
`HitPresented` is `RaySummary/Trace.lean`'s Hit-level hypothesis (family-triple SETS); the trace-level `Presented` is refuted as a completeness carrier by `Probe735.not_presented` and must not be used.

WHY THIS IS THE HIGHEST-INFORMATION PROBE ON THE FRONTIER. The only Hit-level positive, `Probe745.hitPresented_modelA`, is box/stab-free (`Fp`) on a finite-width slice (`Fin 3`); the general row ranges over ⊡-bearing φ and infinite-width models — the two axes along which the refutations live — and nothing in the tree tests `HitPresented` on either. `Node` is both at once. A Hit-level negative would be the first in the tree and would block route R1 at (C); Stage 1 proceeds on R1 alone with no fallback. THAT OUTCOME IS A STAGE 0 USER RULING, NEVER AN AGENT TRANSITION: record it in the probe header, leave the general-row and lift successors to the user, and do not abandon or re-scope them yourself. A positive extends the model-A truncation argument (`kTypeOf_listSeg_replicate_eventually_periodic_bounded`, `inFamily_of_shrink_run`; on `Node` the single-segment containment is a fixture fact as `run_subset_middle` was) to infinite width and ⊡-letters.

GUARDRAILS: (a) the alphabet is `clSig Φ'`, closure-indexed; (b) infinite width, no finiteness added; (c) `L` is a count of states, never a running time or an ∃ L; (d) zero debt — no automata library, no Büchi/MSO citation, no cslib require; (e) forward-only: the header must say nothing here bears on ⊡(Pp), and ⊡-letters are not claimed to discriminate between devices. No sorry, axiom or vacuous definition: if neither headline closes, mark [BLOCKED] with the goal state recorded.

FILE SCOPE. The new probe file; one WIRED entry in `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh`; one sentence in the RaySummary README (durable anchors only, no task numbers). Cross-set collisions left to the admission gate: 734 (script), 744 (README). Serialization edge on T1 (shared script and README), not a logical dependency.

COSTABLE: yes, with a wide band — basis `hit-presented-model-a.lean` (1595 lines; two implement dispatches under --hard per 749's summary, carried-unverified); a negative is cheaper than a positive. Design of record: `specs/757_lplus_decidability_successor_task_set/followup-scope-spec.md` Section B and H2.

  `theorem postCell_stable (k k' j j' : ℕ) (hj : 1 ≤ j) (hj' : 1 ≤ j') (z : ℤ) : cellAt M Φ (.post k j) z = cellAt M Φ (.post k' j') z`  -- the thread index is invisible

### 757. Lplus decidability successor task set
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [757_lplus_decidability_successor_task_set/reports/01_lplus-successor-task-set.md]
- **Plan**: [757_lplus_decidability_successor_task_set/plans/01_lplus-successor-task-set.md]
- **Summary**: [757_lplus_decidability_successor_task_set/summaries/01_lplus-successor-task-set-summary.md]

**Description**: Research, design, and file the successor task set that carries L+ decidability — the unfiled frontier after the (C)/(S)/(W) partial landings

AIM. `Decidable (PlusValidZTime φ)` — decidability of L⁺ over discrete time. This record is the programme-level round that converts the three OPEN frontier obligations into filed, costable task records. It is the successor to the archived programme-review line (`specs/archive/721_decidability_programme_review_l_and_lplus`, `specs/archive/718_omega_sequence_decidability_full_lplus`, `specs/archive/700_lplus_completeness_programme_survey`) and inherits their deliverable format while going one step further: those records explicitly "file nothing", and filing is in scope here by user instruction.

WHY THIS EXISTS. Three successor obligations are named in the tree and filed nowhere. `specs/750_w_run_layer_closure_wide_lift/summaries/01_w-run-layer-ladder-scheme-summary.md`'s Follow-ups section names the first two in its own words — "its own record" for each — and the third falls out of the RaySummary verdict's (S) row:
  (1) RUN-LAYER PHASE 2, soundness half: state and prove stabilization of realized type sets in the deadline counter `c` for `ladderFrame`. No cost figure exists until that lemma is stated.
  (2) RUN-LAYER PHASE 3, completeness half: this IS (C)'s general regular-ℤ row. Open.
  (3) (S) BEYOND ITS LANDED CORNER: `box`/`stab`-bearing `φ`, and every seam but the one.
Re-derive all three against the tree before acting; this description's reading is from 2026-10-10 and the RaySummary README's "The verdict" table is their authority, not this text.

THE LANDED PERIMETER, to be re-verified not trusted. `Compression.decidableValidZTime` decides `ValidZTime` for `Syntax.Formula` only — L, not L⁺ — at `FrameClass.ZTime`, empty premises, axiom profile `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. L⁺ needs its own route because (C5) `StabFaithful` is not stateable over `Formula`. (C) landed its probe half (`Probe745.hitPresented_modelA`) and its bound half ON ONE FIXTURE as a cell count (`presentationSize k = Nat.card (KType (clSig Fp) k)`). (S) landed `presented_modelOf` with corollary `hitCl_modelOf_eq_hitOf`, for `hφ : φ.BoxStabFree` at one seam, trace level. (W) landed its anchor layer with the bound `anchorCells_ncard_le` (`≤ 2 ^ (2 ^ |cl φ|)`, a count of cells) and its run-layer frame scheme `ladderFrame`/`ofSlicedStepFinBranch` (obligation (i) regularity part, obligation (iii) discharged, depth `k = 1`); obligation (ii) is open in both halves.

MACHINE-CHECKED NEGATIVES THAT CONSTRAIN EVERY PROPOSAL. The finite-carrier finite model property is REFUTED, unconditionally and not rescued by the CTL-like fragment. `Presented` is REFUTED as a completeness carrier by `Probe735.not_presented`, for every finite state type, labelling and step relation — it is a soundness device only. `exists_tailStable_repr` is refuted by `FixtureStable.lean`. The SLICED finite model property is genuinely open and nothing in the tree bears on it.

ROUTE POSTURE, from `specs/ROADMAP.md` Stage 1. R1 (gluing) ALONE; there is no fallback. R2 (mosaics) is a CONSUMER of R1, not an alternative — deciding mosaics presupposes deciding `⊡`. R4 (filtration) is CLOSED by `no_finite_carrier_sat` and `not_finite_width_fmp`; cite those library names. R5 (FO translation) is low value and routes back to R1. None of these verdicts is to be re-litigated by this record.

GUARDRAILS every filed successor must satisfy, carried from the ROADMAP's own list. (a) The substrate works over the TIME AXIS with a finite alphabet of per-time summaries, never over states, and that alphabet is CLOSURE-INDEXED — the depth-2 value is machine-checked not a function of the depth-1 stratum, so any phase assuming one bit per depth reproduces a refuted shape. (b) Gluing does not bound the fibre; any phase reintroducing a finite-fibre or finite-width assumption reproduces a refuted shape. (c) NO UPPER BOUND is claimed anywhere — every quantity in the tree is a count of cells, never a running time, and the held 2EXPTIME ceiling rules proposals out rather than supplying an upper bound. (d) ZERO DEBT: no external Büchi/MSO lemma cited in place of a proof, and no `require` on `~/Projects/cslib/` (read-only reference; a require edge drags the tree 76 Mathlib commits forward). (e) Two limits on claims: the `⊡(Pp)` backward side is fixed only by `Probe719Backward.pastStab_iff_allBwdPathsMeet` with no backward Bool-fixture bridge, so a forward-only result must say so rather than implying symmetry; and `⊡(Fp)`/`⊡(Pp)` are device-inert on finite fixtures, so no filed record may claim they discriminate between devices.

SCOPE. (1) Re-verify the three obligations' current status against the tree by declaration name, recording the compile record the way the precedent format does (import `FormalSystem`, print types and axiom profiles). (2) Design the decomposition: for each obligation decide whether it is one record or several, what its first statement is, which depth `k` it fixes, and what its dependency edges and `file_scope` are — the latter matters because the precedent notes the run-layer and bound-half records serialize through their shared `RaySummary/README.md` and `docs/theorem-index.md` territory rather than through a dependency edge. (3) Write a followup-scope-spec in the precedent format (`specs/archive/721_.../followup-scope-spec.md`), including a Section 0 verification record re-checking every anchor cited. (4) FILE the successor tasks — this is the step the precedent records deliberately omit and the reason this record exists. Each filed task must carry a declared `file_scope`, a per-phase `Files to modify` plan when it reaches planning, and a statement shape rather than a prose goal. (5) Record which obligations are NOT costable yet and why, rather than inventing a figure.

NON-GOALS, stated so they are not swept in. No attempt on the mathematics itself — this record researches, designs and files; it proves no obligation. No re-attempt of R4 or R5. No fourth certificate class: no class presenting finite per-time fibres is complete whatever its clauses, both halves of that obstruction being landed under `PlusSlicedCertificate/Limits/`. No upper-bound claim. No transition of the Stage 0 tasks reserved for user rulings — the `[BLOCKED]` marker correction, the re-ruling on the CTL-like fragment record's value, the held 2EXPTIME ceiling, and the `[PARTIAL]` mosaics route are the user's to rule on and this record may describe but never move them. No edit to the sliced-certificate sub-front already filed as its own live tasks.

The followup-scope-spec lives under `specs/**` so task-number citation is permitted there; any edit this record makes to a deliverable file outside `specs/**` (e.g. a README or `docs/theorem-index.md`) must carry no task-number references and no record-versioning language, citing durable anchors — declaration names, file names, section headings — only.

### 756. Repin lem nullity after paper strengthening
- **Effort**: small
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: paper-alignment
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Re-pin `lem:nullity` in the paper definitions of record after the JPL paper strengthened it from an implication to a biconditional, and correct the in-tree docstrings that quote the retired wording verbatim.

THE DRIFT. `scripts/check-paper-definitions.sh` reports exactly one drifted anchor against `docs/reference/paper-definitions-of-record.md`:

  OLD (pinned, sha256 94ed018343635a8ef6671daef07eaa72da1cb49fd11043fb3aa9b391a2c9c973):
    $w \Rightarrow_0 w$ for every world state $w \in W$ in every task frame $\F = \tuple{W, \D, \Rightarrow}$.
  NEW (live paper, sha256 8442e2bf6b5f6515f2f906132bbe15fe919973d82ae573dba29a919ad14636f5):
    $w \Rightarrow_0 u$ if and only if $w = u$, for all world states $w, u \in W$ in any task frame $\F$.

The new statement is strictly STRONGER: an implication became a biconditional characterising `⇒₀` as the identity relation.

THE FORMALIZATION IS NOT BEHIND THE PAPER. This is the load-bearing fact for scoping, and it is verified, not assumed: the tree already carries BOTH forms. `FormalSystem/Semantics.lean:257` names them together — the field `FrameOver.nullity` (the weak loop) and the biconditional `FrameOver.nullity_identity`. `FormalSystem/Semantics/IntNormalForm.lean:24` already describes `⇒₀` as the identity and cites `FrameOver.nullity_identity` as the derived theorem for it, and `IntNormalForm.lean:200` discharges a base case with `F.nullity_identity w u`. So no new Lean proof is required by this drift; the record is simply stale relative to a paper the library already over-satisfies.

NOT A CI FAILURE. `check-paper-definitions.sh`'s own usage block documents that an absent paper file is a neutral skip with exit 0, and names that as the CI path, since CI cannot see the paper's repository — confirmed by running it with a nonexistent `--paper`. The gate is red only on a machine where the paper is checked out alongside this repo. It is therefore a local-correctness obligation, not a release blocker.

SCOPE. (1) Re-pin the `lem:nullity` block and its sha256 in `docs/reference/paper-definitions-of-record.md`, following that file's own dated drift-correction convention for an amendment to a decision the record owns; `check-paper-definitions.sh --resolve` is the sanctioned way to obtain the new text and hash rather than transcribing by hand (there is no `--fix` mode, by design). (2) Correct the in-tree sites that quote the RETIRED wording. Four of them label the quotation "verbatim" and so become false on re-pin: `FormalSystem/Semantics/TaskFrame.lean:113` and `:919`, `FormalSystem/Semantics/FrameAxioms.lean:65`, `FormalSystem/Semantics/Extension/Admissible.lean:34`. (3) Review the sites that paraphrase the weak form without claiming verbatim quotation and decide case by case whether each should now cite the biconditional: `FormalSystem/Semantics.lean:255`, `FormalSystem/Semantics/Presheaf/Behavior.lean:91`, `FormalSystem/Semantics/FrameAxioms.lean:113`, `FormalSystem/Semantics/Extension/Admissible.lean:117`, `FormalSystem/Semantics/Extension/Extension.lean:171`, `docs/reference/state-topology-appendix-support.md:103`. Several are legitimate as they stand — a proof that needs only the weak loop should keep citing the weak loop — so this step is a judgment pass, not a search-and-replace. (4) Preserve the record's standing claim that Nullity is DERIVED and not an axiom (`paper-definitions-of-record.md:976`, `TaskFrame.lean:113`): the strengthening does not promote it to an axiom.

ACCEPTANCE. `scripts/check-paper-definitions.sh` exits 0 against the live paper; `lake build` green; `scripts/readme-lint.sh` and `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh --no-build` still exit 0; no docstring claims a verbatim quotation it does not reproduce.

NON-GOALS: no new Lean declarations — `nullity_identity` already exists; no change to the four frame axioms or to Nullity's derived status; no edit to the paper itself.

### 755. Plan files to modify unenforced
- **Effort**: small
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [755_plan_files_to_modify_unenforced/reports/01_files-to-modify-unenforced.md]
- **Plan**: [755_plan_files_to_modify_unenforced/plans/01_files-to-modify-enforcement.md]
- **Summary**: [755_plan_files_to_modify_unenforced/summaries/01_files-to-modify-enforcement-summary.md]

**Description**: A plan artifact can omit the `Files to modify` field that `plan-format.md` marks REQUIRED, pass validation with zero warnings, and leave the task's `file_scope` null — which makes the task invisible to the admission overlap gate.

EVIDENCE (all three verified mechanically, not inferred):

1. `context/formats/plan-format.md:108` declares `- **Files to modify:** (required)` as a per-phase field, and line 149 freezes the heading text because three independent consumers embed it.
2. `scripts/validate-artifact.sh` contains NO check for the field — a grep for `files to modify` / `files_to_modify` over the whole script returns zero hits. A plan missing every occurrence of a REQUIRED field therefore validates clean.
3. `scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` returns `[]` when no `Files to modify` block is present, and its own header comment (line 15) classifies that as a normal outcome rather than an error. The union-merge in `update-task-status.sh` then adds nothing, leaving `file_scope` as it was — `null` for a task that never had one.

LIVE INSTANCE: task #410 (`internalize_tableau_branches_and_prove_routine_rule_admissibility`) is at status `planned` with `file_scope: null`. Its plan `plans/01_internalize-routine-admissibility.md` contains zero `Files to modify` headings. The harvest ran at plan postflight and correctly returned `[]`; nothing anywhere reported that a required field was missing.

POPULATION: 25 of 55 active tasks carry a null or empty `file_scope`. Most are `not_started` and have no plan yet, so they are not evidence of this defect; #410 (`planned`) is the clean instance, and #219 (`researched`) has no plan artifact at all. The 25-task figure is context for how large the gate's blind spot currently is, not a claim that all 25 share this cause.

WHY THIS MATTERS: `file_scope` is what the orchestrator's admission gate uses to detect territory collisions between tasks. A null scope is not "no collisions" — it is "collisions undetectable". The in-batch defer that correctly serialized two recent tasks only fired because both declared the overlapping path.

SCOPE: (1) Decide where the missing-required-field check belongs — `validate-artifact.sh`'s plan mode is the obvious site, since it already runs at plan postflight and already has a warning channel. (2) Decide the tier: a plan with no `Files to modify` in any phase is a different severity from one where a single phase omits it; the field is per-phase. (3) Decide whether `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` should distinguish "no block found anywhere" (suspicious) from "block found, lists no paths" (legitimate, e.g. a pure-verification phase) — today both return `[]`. (4) Backfill #410's `file_scope` from its plan's actual content as the regression fixture, and confirm a re-run of the harvest on a repaired plan produces a non-empty scope.

NON-GOALS: no change to the frozen `Files to modify` heading text or its grammar; no change to the union-merge semantics in `update-task-status.sh` (that is a separate record's territory); no retroactive scope backfill across all 25 null-scope tasks, most of which simply have no plan yet.

RELATION TO ADJACENT RECORDS: distinct from the record covering generated surfaces absent from the harvested set (that one assumes the block exists and is incomplete; this one is the block being absent entirely), and distinct from the record covering the union-merge's silence on the write path (that one is observability of a merge that happened; this one is a merge that never had input). The edit target is the SOURCE STORE mirroring these deployed paths, resolved via `.claude-extensions.json`'s `source_dir`, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. The deployed `.claude/` tree was reported STALE for the `core` extension when this was found — redeploy via `deploy-headless.sh` before reasoning from the deployed copy.
