### 754. plan postflight widens file_scope silently
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [754_plan_postflight_file_scope_widening_silent/reports/01_file_scope_widening_notice.md]
- **Plan**: [754_plan_postflight_file_scope_widening_silent/plans/01_file-scope-widening-notice.md]
- **Summary**: [754_plan_postflight_file_scope_widening_silent/summaries/01_file-scope-widening-notice-summary.md]

**Description**: Make the plan-postflight file_scope widening observable instead of silent

READ THIS FIRST -- THE ORIGINATING CHANGE REQUEST'S PREMISE WAS WRONG, AND THE RESIDUE IS NARROW. The request that produced this record asked for "a reconciliation step so that a plan's enumerated 'Files to modify' set is compared against the task's declared file_scope at plan postflight, and the divergence is either harvested into file_scope or surfaced". The harvest-and-widen half ALREADY EXISTS AND ALREADY WORKS; do not rebuild it. `scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` unions every phase's `Files to modify` list from the latest plan artifact and both live plan-postflight paths forward it as `--file-scope-add=<json>` to `scripts/update-task-status.sh`, whose jq clause `(.file_scope) |= ((. // []) + $add | unique)` is an ADDITIVE union-merge onto whatever the task already declared. Verified against the originating evidence: the task that triggered the request declared a 5-path file_scope at research postflight and a 10-path one at plan postflight (`git log -- specs/state.json`, commit subject `create implementation plan`), and the harvester re-run on that task's own plan reproduces 9 of those 10 paths exactly -- including every one of the four files the issue-log entry said were missing. The reconciliation fired and resolved the gap, which is precisely what that entry's own closing sentence predicted. Re-derive this before acting; it is a measurement, not a contract.

THE ACTUAL DEFECT: THE WIDENING IS UNANNOUNCED. `update-task-status.sh` echoes the merge ONLY under `--dry-run` ("state.json: file_scope union-merge -> add ..."). On the real write path -- both the `state_is_noop` arm and the main PHASE 3 clause -- the union-merge runs with no output at all: no count, no path list, no record that the task's declared scope changed. A plan postflight can silently double a task's file_scope and nothing in the run log says so. Consequences, in descending order of concreteness. (1) An operator cannot tell a correctly-widened scope from a scope widened by a mis-parsed or over-broad plan list, because neither prints. (2) The planner on the originating task had to hand-write an issue-log entry to document a divergence the machinery was absorbing anyway -- manual bookkeeping standing in for a notice the tooling could emit for free. (3) `file_scope` is the admission gate's overlap-collision surface (`orchestrate-batch-admit.sh`), so a silent widening silently enlarges the territory a task collides on, changing later batch-admission outcomes with no trace of when or why it grew.

DECIDE AND LAND ONE OF THREE, OR A COMPOSITION. (a) Emit a non-blocking notice from `update-task-status.sh` whenever `FILE_SCOPE_ADD_LEN > 0` on the real write path, naming the count actually added (the post-merge delta, NOT the raw forwarded list -- `unique` means most forwarded paths are usually already present, so a notice that prints the input would cry wolf on every postflight). This is the smallest honest fix and it covers every caller at once, which is why it is preferred. (b) Have the two live call sites announce it instead -- `scripts/skill-base.sh`'s plan arm and `scripts/orchestrator-postflight.sh`'s Stage 7 branch. Weaker: it duplicates the message in two places and the second has no live callers, so it is the wrong altitude for a behavior that belongs to the writer. (c) Record the widening durably rather than only printing it -- e.g. a per-task note of what plan postflight added. Heaviest; justify it against (a) before choosing it, and only if a consumer for the record actually exists. Whichever is chosen, the delta-not-input rule in (a) is binding: a notice that fires on every postflight regardless of whether anything changed is worse than silence, because it trains operators to ignore it.

NON-GOALS, EXPLICITLY. No change to the harvest grammar or to `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` -- that file is the adjacent generated-surfaces record's territory, not this one's. No change to the union-merge SEMANTICS (additive, `unique`, never overwrite) -- only to its observability. No new gate, no blocking behavior, no exit-code effect: this is a notice, and a notice that can fail a postflight is a regression. No change to the implement-postflight excursion predicate in `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`, whose documented ruling is drop-and-warn, never refuse.

WHAT THIS RECORD IS NOT. The four paths actually reported as EXCURSION DROPPED on the originating run (root `README.md`, `FormalSystem/README.md`, `FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md`, `typst/generated/status.typ`) are a DISJOINT set from the four the issue-log entry named, and they are generated surfaces -- the subject of the existing `harvest_generated_surfaces_into_file_scope` record, which already names three of those four by name and already instructs its implementer to find the rest by reading the generators. `FormalSystem/README.md` is the one not named there and falls inside that record's stated method. Do not re-scope generated-surface harvesting here, and do not treat those drops as evidence for this record's defect; they are evidence for that one.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. Every file_scope path below lives in a deployed, regenerated tree; a hand edit under `.claude/**` is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Implementation MUST resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring paths there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. The deployed paths are declared in file_scope only so the overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are not the edit target.

DEPLOYED TREE WAS STALE. This repository's deployed `.claude/` tree was reported STALE relative to the source store for the `core` extension throughout the originating run. Redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean` BEFORE reasoning about current behavior from the deployed copy, and again after editing the source store.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. The edited files are not under `specs/**`, so their prose must carry no task-number references and no record-versioning language; cite durable anchors (file names, function names, section headings, field names) only.

RELATED RECORDS, FOR ORIENTATION AND NOT AS DEPENDENCY EDGES. The generated-surfaces harvest record covers `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` and the `.lean`-forced derived surfaces. The file_scope-declaration-hygiene record covers coarse and absent declarations feeding the same admission gate. No file-territory overlap with either (this record touches the status writer and its call sites, not the harvester or the validator), so no dependency edge; if the generated-surfaces record lands first, the notice built here simply reports its additions too.

### 753. conclusion stage drops null-resolution issue-log entries
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Close the contract gap that makes null-resolution issue-log entries invisible to the conclusion stage

THE CONCLUSION STAGE SILENTLY DROPS NULL-RESOLUTION ISSUE-LOG ENTRIES. `context/formats/issue-log.md` declares the `resolution` field optional and nullable (its field table row reads "no (nullable)", closed vocabulary `fixed_inline | worked_around | open`). `context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Filter Predicate" admits an entry into channel classification ONLY when `resolution` is `open` or `worked_around`. An entry written with `resolution: null` is therefore neither closed (`fixed_inline`, which legitimately generates no channel item) nor classified -- it reaches NO channel and is invisible to the user. Both documents are internally consistent; the gap is between them.

VERIFIED LIVE, NOT HYPOTHESIZED. `specs/749_c_bound_half_model_a_presentation_size/issues.jsonl` holds 11 entries, every one with `resolution: null` and `suggested_channel: null`. `bash .claude/scripts/orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh --aggregate --session <sid> --tasks 749` returned `proposals: 0`, `wins: 4`, `unknown_classes: 6`. Of the 11 entries, 7 are `kind: "issue"` and were all dropped: six at `severity: minor` (classes `stale-citation`, `plan-deviation`, `plan-gap`, `plan-gap`, `plan-gap`, `build-failure`) and one at `severity: costly` (class `plan-deviation`). The 4 `kind: "win"` entries surfaced correctly, which confirms the aggregator is behaving exactly as written -- this is a contract mismatch, not an aggregator bug. Re-derive these counts from the live log rather than trusting them; they are a snapshot.

DECIDE AND LAND ONE OF THREE. (a) Make `resolution` REQUIRED at write time in `scripts/issue-record.sh`, so an entry cannot be recorded without one -- closes the hole at the writer, but every existing null-resolution entry across the repo stays invisible. (b) Widen the Filter Predicate to treat null-or-absent `resolution` as `open` -- the conservative reading, since an issue recorded without a resolution is an unresolved one; note this alone would have pushed 7 entries from ONE task into channel classification, so weigh proposal volume before choosing it. (c) Keep both contracts as they are and have the conclusion stage report a separate "unclassifiable (no resolution)" tally beside its unknown-class tally, so the entries are at least visible rather than dropped. Options (a) and (c) are composable; (b) subsumes neither. Whichever is chosen, the two documents MUST end up agreeing in writing -- the acceptance condition for this record is that a reader of either file can predict the other's behavior.

ALSO IN SCOPE. `suggested_channel` was null on all 7 dropped entries too. `conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Hint-Versus-Decision Rule" treats that field as a hint the orchestrator may override visibly, which presumes a hint exists; decide whether a null hint is acceptable or whether the writer should require one, and say so in the same place the `resolution` decision is recorded.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. All four file_scope paths live in a deployed, regenerated tree -- a hand edit under `.claude/**` is wiped by the next deploy and the edit will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Implementation MUST resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring paths there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`, then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The deployed paths are declared in file_scope only so the overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are not the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. The edited files are not under `specs/**`, so the prose must carry no task-number references and no record-versioning language; cite durable anchors (file names, section headings, field names) only.

RELATED RECORDS, FOR ORIENTATION AND NOT AS DEPENDENCY EDGES. The roadmap-generator record covering `roadmap-integration.sh`'s partial-write behavior is the sibling core-extension defect with the same source-store boundary, and the file_scope-declaration-hygiene record covers a different blindness in the same admission machinery. No dependency edge to either -- nothing here blocks on them.

Note that a reference to a roadmap-tooling task circulates in other records without resolving to an existing one; filing this is what makes that reference resolve.

It does NOT assemble the `## Open work` / `## Next` layout, and it does not touch the authored Stage prose. So the defect to land is narrower than "silently inoperative": the script now half-applies to an authored layout, emitting its only diagnostic on stderr as an HTML comment rather than a named message, and appending generated content into a file whose own header declares it authored-and-not-generated. The authored-only-versus-teach-the-layout decision this record asks for is unchanged and still the thing to settle; what changed is that a caller now gets exit 0 and a partial write instead of a silent exit 1, which is a different failure to describe and a different one to fix. Re-verify against the live script before implementing; the exit-1/zero-output description predates the redeploy.

### 750. W run layer closure wide lift
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [750_w_run_layer_closure_wide_lift/reports/01_w-run-layer-design.md]
- **Plan**: [750_w_run_layer_closure_wide_lift/plans/01_w-run-layer-ladder-scheme.md]
- **Summary**: [750_w_run_layer_closure_wide_lift/summaries/01_w-run-layer-ladder-scheme-summary.md]

**Description**: **(W)'s RUN LAYER — ITS OWN RECORD, AS `RaySummary/README.md` AND 747's follow-up both instruct.** (W)'s anchor layer is landed and bounded in `AnchorLayer.lean`; the run layer — the `untl`/`snce` nesting and the lift from one seam to `¬ PlusValidZTime φ` — is BLOCKED on three named obligations, each with its source and none with a measured cost (`README.md:378-383`). Obligation (iii) is the presentation-size bound and is a SEPARATE record's phase 1, not this one's.

**PHASE 1 (the design question only; DISPATCH STOPS HERE).** Thread-indexed unfolding versus a bounded-deadline encoding, for a frame-scheme constructor producing infinite-width, finitely branching regular frames. Inputs: the refuted repair shapes `Rbad` (`b2b-frame-constructor-branching.lean:178`) and `Rok`, whose positive halves `fib_finite_of_finBranch` (`:147`) and `saturation_of_finBranch` (`:169`) need TWO-SIDED finite branching; and the `Node` thread-unfolding shape (`PlusSlicedCertificate/Limits/NoFiniteWidth.lean:132`), which is neither refuted nor built. Phase 1's outcome is what makes phases 2-3 costable; this record says so rather than pricing them. **Phase 1 also outputs the depth `k` the run layer needs**, since the bound record lands its result for every `k` and chooses none. **INFINITE WIDTH IS FORCED**, by `not_finite_width_fmp` (`Limits/NoFiniteWidth.lean:1280`) and concretely by `Probe747.modelOf_incomplete` (`w-soundness-shape-refuted.lean:131`); any phase reintroducing a finite-width or finite-fibre assumption reproduces a refuted shape. The ~120-160 line figure is CARRIED, UNVERIFIED from (S) and covers the regularity part only (`README.md:368-370`); the unfolding design is unmeasured and this record states that it has no cost figure.

**PHASE 2 — OBLIGATION (ii), SOUNDNESS HALF (object → model).** A Hit-level realization theorem for phase 1's constructor: the analogue of `presented_modelOf` (`Realize.lean`, the (S) realization theorem that discharged `Presented` for `modelOf`) at `HitPresented` level, PER CELL. The `stab` case of the constructor's truth lemma needs the built model to be `HitPresented` at every cell by the object's per-cell presentation. It must be Hit-level or product-level per cell — `HitPresented`, family-triple SETS — and NEVER trace-level `Presented`, because `stab_iff_of_presented_nowUnique` (`w-soundness-shape-refuted.lean:188`) shows trace level collapses `ψ ↔ ⊡ψ` at any `Presented`+`nowUnique` seam; see `w-soundness-shape-refuted.lean:44-49` for the consequence as the probe states it.

**PHASE 3 — OBLIGATION (ii), COMPLETENESS HALF (model → object) = (C)'s GENERAL ROW.** Every refuting model must yield an object whose per-cell presentations present its cells. Stated verbatim from `RaySummary/README.md:154`: "For every regular `ℤ`-model `M`, seam `(τ, t)`, formula `φ` and depth `1 ≤ k`, a finite labelled presentation `(Q, lab, step)` with `Fintype.card Q` below a computable bound in `φ`, such that `HitCl M τ t φ k = ↑(hitOf lab step (clNow φ) k)`". This is OPEN MATHEMATICS with ONE positive fixture and no general argument: `Probe735.not_presented` refutes the TRACE-level version (which is why the Hit level is the only one left), `Probe745.hitPresented_modelA` is the only Hit-level positive, and a grep for `¬ HitPresented` / `not_hitPresented` over `specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/*.lean` and `RaySummary/` returns ZERO — there is no Hit-level negative anywhere. No cost figure, and this record states that none exists.

**SEMI-DECIDABILITY CAVEAT, carried verbatim from `README.md:380-385` and binding on this record's acceptance**: "Without (iii), a landed composition theorem would read `¬ PlusValidZTime φ ↔ ∃ object, …` over an UNBOUNDED run layer — at best semi-decidable — and NOT `Decidable (PlusValidZTime φ)`." `Decidable (PlusValidZTime φ)` must appear in no statement of this record.

**DOCUMENTATION DELIVERABLE (one paragraph, no new task).** Record in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/README.md`'s (W) section that the obstruction (W) names has a literature description: the quasimodel-realisation step of Hodkinson-Wolter-Zakharyaschev 2000 Def. 12 and Gabbay-Kurucz-Wolter-Zakharyaschev 2003 Thm 13.6 presupposes a freely chosen state function and run set — HWZ Remark 13 (PDF p.11) makes run choice free in both — whereas TM's quasistate at every time is the type set realised by the history set (the SAME set at every time, by shift closure) and its runs are the task-respecting paths of ONE time-invariant relation whose states carry both the atoms and every `stab` formula (`stab_state_only`, `PlusTruth.lean:332`). That is the condition under which published quasimodel realisation stops applying, and it is exactly what (W)'s soundness half is about. DELIVERABLE-HYGIENE: this file is NOT `specs/**`, so the paragraph must carry no task-number references and no record-versioning language; cite durable anchors (file names, theorem names, author-year citations, section headings) only.

**NO DEPENDENCY EDGE to the bound record.** An edge would assert the run layer cannot proceed without the bound; `README.md:380-385` says it can, yielding a weaker (semi-decidable) statement. Serialization comes from the `file_scope` overlap both records declare on `RaySummary/README.md` and `docs/theorem-index.md`; ordering comes from the roadmap's Run block. The gate serializes, the roadmap orders, and no record claims a blocking relation that does not exist.

**BINDING CONSTRAINTS.** No complexity bound, upper or lower, anywhere; the ARGUED CTL⋆ 2EXPTIME ceiling is never an upper-bound source. Never write that L⁺ is decidable or undecidable unqualified — carry frame class, fragment, premise set and hypothesis, `[F.IsRegular]` at minimum. File no fourth certificate class: no class presenting finite per-time fibres is complete whatever its clauses, and both halves of that obstruction are landed under `PlusSlicedCertificate/Limits/`. Sorry-free completion required; `[BLOCKED]` with the obstruction named is the honest outcome for a route that does not close.

This record owns the bound **on the fixture**. The general every-model statement is OUT OF SCOPE here and is the completeness half of the (W) run-layer record.

**PHASE 1 (hours; closed in principle).** Land, in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/WeakCanonical/KTypeSummary/Saturation.lean`, namespace `FormalSystem.Metalogic.WeakCanonical` (matching `kTypeOf_listSeg_append_congr`'s at `Compose.lean:296`), the STRENGTHENED shape `∃ m₀ p, 0 < p ∧ m₀ + p ≤ Nat.card (KType sig k) ∧ ∀ m, m₀ ≤ m → kTypeOf sig k (listSeg (List.replicate (m + p) ℓ)) = kTypeOf sig k (listSeg (List.replicate m ℓ))`, with the shape stated verbatim at `RaySummary/README.md:285-290` as its one-line corollary so the recorded signature stays literally true. ROUTE: **pigeonhole plus `kTypeOf_listSeg_append_congr`** (`Compose.lean:276`), NOT finite-semigroup idempotence — no semigroup is constructed and no idempotent is needed; the congruence is applied once with the first argument varying and the second fixed. Premises: `kType_finite` (`KTypeSummary/Basic.lean:112`), `Finite.exists_ne_map_eq_of_infinite` for the stated shape, `Fintype.exists_ne_map_eq_of_card_lt` on `Fin (Nat.card (KType sig k) + 1)` after `Fintype.ofFinite` and `Nat.card_eq_fintype_card` for the strengthened one, `List.replicate_add`. A feasibility elaboration of both theorems compiled in 2.3 s against the current oleans with axioms `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]` and no `sorryAx`; the scratch text is reproduced in `specs/748_revise_and_create_tasks_lplus_decidability/reports/02_round-two-joint-verification.md`'s Appendix and is FEASIBILITY ONLY — land it here, under this record's `file_scope`, pins and index row, never copied in as a research commit. Keep the stated `[DecidableEq sig.preds]` binder even though the proof does not use it (the `unusedArguments` linter will say so, as `Compose.lean:270-275` predicts for `append_congr`): the stated signature is the recorded one. Pin both theorems in `Tests/BimodalTest/Metalogic/KTypeSummaryAxiomTest.lean` at `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]` (the profile of every (C) pin at `:405-413`); add one `docs/theorem-index.md` row cross-referencing the `anchorCells_ncard_le` row so both counts carry the same disclaimer in the same words. Replace "finite-semigroup idempotence" with "pigeonhole plus `kTypeOf_listSeg_append_congr`" at `RaySummary/README.md:291`, `hit-presented-model-a.lean:97`, and in `append_congr`'s docstring at `Compose.lean:271-275`.

**PHASE 2 (THE COST; UNMEASURED, AND THIS RECORD SAYS SO).** The factorization-transport lemma: `InFamily` is invariant under lengthening a maximal constant run beyond phase 1's threshold — "if `c'` is `c` with a maximal constant run `ℓ^n` replaced by `ℓ^(n+p)`, and `n` is beyond phase 1's threshold, then `InFamily … c ↔ InFamily … c'`". Nothing in the tree states it and no attempt is recorded anywhere; it has NO cost figure and this record asserts none. It is needed because `hitCl_eq_truncated`'s `L` bounds jump OFFSETS over `familyTriples`, a family triple is `InFamily sig k τneg τzero τpos c := ∃ F : SegmentFactorization sig k c, …` (`Family.lean:201-203`), and a `SegmentFactorization` is a monotone covering cutting sequence `W : ℤ → ℤ` (`Family.lean:70-95`) — so phase 1 is about the `k`-type of one finite replicated block and `L` is about cutting sequences of a bi-infinite colouring. The proof is cut surgery (shrink or extend the segment holding the run, with phase 1 and `append_congr` preserving its type) and has a GENUINE CASE SPLIT a plan must face: when every segment meeting the run is short, no single segment can absorb the `p` positions and the factorization must be re-cut. The converse the tree holds, `kTypeOf_zFiber_eq_of_inFamily` (`KTypeSummary/Saturation.lean:133`), is the WRONG DIRECTION for this. State it generally over `sig` first; if that resists, fall back to model A's concrete traces, which by `clTrace_stayA_apply`/`clTrace_jump_apply` (`hit-presented-model-a.lean:470-500`) are `(Fp,¬p)^(ω*) · now · (Fp,¬p)^n · (Fp,p)^ω`. If it does not close: mark the phase `[BLOCKED]` and name the obstruction. NEVER a `sorry`, never a new axiom.

**PHASE 3 (bookkeeping once phase 2 lands).** Exhibit `L` in `hitCl_eq_truncated` (`hit-presented-model-a.lean:593-596`) — `≤ m₀ + p` from phase 1, hence `≤ Nat.card (KType (clSig Fp) k)` — replacing the non-constructive `Set.Finite.bddAbove` witness at `:603`. Exhibiting `L` CHANGES `hitCl_eq_truncated`'s statement, so revise the `∃ L` prefix of `Probe745.hitPresented_modelA` and its "What `hitPresented_modelA` does NOT say" block (`:1303-1312`) in the SAME change rather than leaving them asserting non-exhibition; then re-run `bash scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh`.

**NON-NEGOTIABLES.** (1) `Nat.card (KType …)` is described as a COUNT and nothing else. Quote, verbatim, `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/AnchorLayer.lean:317-320` wherever this record's count appears: "The one bound (W) can state honestly: a cardinality bound on the SET OF CELLS of a model, syntactic in `|cl φ|` — a cell is a set of subsets of `cl φ`, so there are at most `2 ^ (2 ^ |cl φ|)` of them. It is not a complexity bound of any kind: it counts cells, it does not time anything, and it must not be described as a bound on any procedure's running time." Derive nothing from the size of `NormalForm (clSig φ) k 0`. (2) The alphabet constraint 739 left standing is already met by the tree and is not re-litigated: `clSig φ := subformulas φ ⊕ Unit` (`Closure.lean:80-81`) and `subformulas` carries both `untl` and `snce` arguments (`Subformulas.lean:66-73`), so backward labels are letters by construction. (3) `k` STAYS FREE: land the bound for every `k` and choose none. `k = 1` suffices for the `⊡` bridge alone (`plusStab_iff_forall_hitSig`, `plusStab_iff_forall_hitCl`, both `(hk : 1 ≤ k)` at `Trace.lean:240`; `hitPresented_modelA` at `hit-presented-model-a.lean:1282`); what depth the `untl`/`snce` run layer needs is the (W) record's phase-1 output, not this record's. (4) `Decidable (PlusValidZTime φ)` appears in no statement. (5) No complexity bound, upper or lower, is asserted or implied anywhere; the ARGUED CTL⋆ 2EXPTIME ceiling is never an upper-bound source. ### 744. Wrap inventory tables generated markers
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: markdown
- **Topic**: repo-hygiene
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Wrap the RaySummary and KTypeSummary inventory tables in BEGIN GENERATED markers so their module line counts cannot drift again

MEASURED 2026-10-08. Four of the six module line counts in FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/README.md's inventory table had drifted from the filesystem: Closure.lean claimed 310 (actual 309), Trace.lean 336 (actual 371), Compose.lean 297 (actual 298), Present.lean 684 (actual 690). HitOf.lean (810) and StabCheck.lean (252) were correct, as was the #guard count (13 + 4 = 17). The four stale counts were corrected BY HAND in that session's commit; this task removes the recurrence, which the hand fix does not.

ROOT CAUSE, verified by grep. That table carries NO `<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: inventory dir=... -->` / `<!-- END GENERATED -->` markers, so it is hand-maintained and `check-module-invariants.sh`'s INV check -- which fails only on a STALE WRAPPED BLOCK -- never covered it. The gate was green throughout the drift. scripts/readme-inventory.sh is a deprecated shim whose own message states the mechanism: "Module inventory tables are no longer pasted in by hand. A README opts into a machine-owned table by wrapping it in BEGIN GENERATED ... after which `check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory` rewrites every column except the hand-written trailing description, and the INV check in the same script fails if any block has gone stale."

SO THE FIX IS OPT-IN, NOT NEW MACHINERY: wrap the table, and the existing generator owns the columns while the long hand-written trailing descriptions survive. Confirm that preservation claim against `--emit-inventory`'s actual behaviour before committing to it -- the RaySummary descriptions are several hundred words each and losing them would be a serious regression, so verify on a scratch copy first.

SCOPE. (1) Wrap the RaySummary README inventory table and regenerate it via `check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory`, confirming every trailing description is preserved byte-for-byte. (2) Do the same for FormalSystem/Metalogic/WeakCanonical/KTypeSummary/README.md, which has the same hand-maintained shape. (3) Sweep for OTHER unwrapped inventory tables under FormalSystem/ by reading the generator's marker-option list rather than enumerating from memory, and report what is found -- wrapping them is in scope only where the generator's column set actually fits. (4) Confirm `check-module-invariants.sh` INV now fails on a deliberately corrupted count in each newly wrapped block, so the coverage is demonstrated rather than assumed.

DEPENDENCY NOTE, not a declared edge. Task 731 (make plan-time file_scope harvest include the generated surfaces a .lean edit forces) enumerates the generated surfaces a planner must declare. If this task lands, these two READMEs BECOME such surfaces and 731's enumeration must include them. 731 is NOT declared as a dependency here and this task is not blocked on it; the relationship runs the other way, so 731 should be re-checked after this lands. Do not edit 731's record from this task.

WHY THIS IS NOT FOLDED INTO 731. Task 731's NON-GOALS state explicitly: "no change to the generators themselves or to what they emit". Wrapping a new table changes what the generator emits (it would own two more tables), so this work is excluded by 731's own stated scope. Folding it in would contradict that record rather than extend it.

NON-GOALS. No change to the generator's column set or algorithm. No change to any module's Lean source. Do not hand-correct counts in any table this task does not wrap -- an unwrapped table left unwrapped should be REPORTED, not patched, so the recurrence stays visible rather than being papered over a second time.

BACKLOG RECONCILIATION, verified 2026-10-08 against all 51 open tasks in this repo. No open task covers inventory-table marker adoption. 731 is adjacent and explicitly excludes it (above). 741 concerns agent-system context extensions, not this repo's READMEs. No overlap.

### 743. Typst sync check1 namespace resolution
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: general
- **Topic**: repo-hygiene
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Teach typst-sync-check.sh Check 1 to resolve namespaced Lean declarations, or rule on the whitelist-vs-docstring convention it currently depends on

MEASURED 2026-10-08. `bash scripts/typst-sync-check.sh` FAILS Check 1 (backtick name resolution) on exactly one finding: VIOLATION `FormalSystem.Semantics.validZTime_iff_validInt` -- "identifier not found in any *.lean file under the Lean source roots", cited from typst/FormalFoundations.typ:790. Three separate implementation dispatches during an /orchestrate run reported it and each correctly declined to repair it as out of scope.

THE CITATION IS CORRECT; THE CHECK CANNOT RESOLVE IT. `theorem validZTime_iff_validInt` is declared at FormalSystem/Semantics/IntTransfer.lean:338, inside `namespace FormalSystem.Semantics` (opened line 70, closed line 353), so the fully-qualified name the manual cites is the right name. Check 1's dotted-identifier branch does a LITERAL grep for the full dotted string and performs no namespace resolution, so a correctly-cited namespaced declaration fails unless its full dotted name happens to appear verbatim somewhere in a .lean file.

WHY THAT IS FRAGILE, measured rather than asserted. Every other fully-qualified FormalSystem.* citation sampled from the typst tree DOES get a literal hit (FormalSystem.Metalogic.BXCanonical, FormalSystem.Metalogic.Conservativity, FormalSystem.Metalogic.Decidability.Compression.decidableValidZTime, FormalSystem.ProofSystem, FormalSystem.ProofSystem.Axioms, FormalSystem.ProofSystem.DerivedAxioms) -- because the name appears in a namespace declaration or a docstring cross-reference, not because the check resolved anything. The manual's citations are therefore validated by coincidence of source text: a correct citation passes or fails depending on whether some docstring mentions it.

THREE ROUTES, none obviously right, to be decided rather than assumed. (1) Teach Check 1 to resolve a dotted name against open namespaces -- correct, but the script is deliberately BUILD-FREE (no lake, no oleans, so it can run in a fast pre-commit gate), so resolution without a build means parsing namespace/end nesting. (2) Whitelist the name in typst/sync-check-whitelist.txt -- the sanctioned escape hatch, BUT it holds 89 entries and ZERO dotted FormalSystem.* entries, and its documented categories are "type-signature / turnstile illustrations" and "typst/template API names (not Lean identifiers)", so a valid-but-unresolvable declaration would set a new precedent and mask a real gap. (3) Change the manual to cite the short name, which does appear literally -- cheapest, but loses the fully-qualified form in a reference manual and does not stop the next namespaced citation from failing.

A FOURTH, if route 1 is rejected: add the fully-qualified cross-reference to IntTransfer.lean's own module docstring, making the citation resolve the same way the other six do. Cost: touching a .lean file changes formalsystem-line-count (347522 at measurement) in typst/generated/status.typ, forcing a status.typ regeneration, and that docstring's existing bullet list uses SHORT names throughout, so a dotted entry would be stylistically inconsistent there.

ACCEPTANCE. Either Check 1 resolves the citation and `bash scripts/typst-sync-check.sh` exits 0, with the chosen mechanism's limits stated where the next citation author will see them; or a recorded verdict naming which route was taken and why the others were rejected, with the finding left visible rather than silently suppressed. If the whitelist route is chosen, say explicitly that it is suppressing a resolution gap and not certifying the name.

NON-GOALS. No change to Check 2 (status.typ counts), Check 2b (module map) or Check 3 (machine appendix). No change to what any generator emits. Do not make the script require a built library -- its build-free property is load-bearing for the pre-commit gate.

BACKLOG RECONCILIATION, verified 2026-10-08 against all 51 open tasks in this repo. No open task covers Check 1 or phantom-citation resolution. Nearest records: 731 (plan-time file_scope harvest must include generated surfaces a .lean edit forces -- adjacent because it also concerns typst-sync-check.sh, but its subject is scope DECLARATION and its NON-GOALS explicitly exclude generator changes) and 741 (agent-system context extensions; mentions typst-sync staleness only as deploy-freshness context). Neither overlaps this.

UPDATE 2026-10-10: THE REPRO ABOVE NO LONGER FIRES; THE MECHANISM GAP IS UNCHANGED.

`scripts/typst-sync-check.sh` now exits 0 (all four checks green). That is NOT because Check 1 learned namespace resolution — it is because the single citation was rewritten at `typst/FormalFoundations.typ:790` from the fully-qualified `FormalSystem.Semantics.validZTime_iff_validInt` to the module-path-plus-leaf form `` `validZTime_iff_validInt` in `Semantics/IntTransfer.lean` ``, which resolves on both halves under the existing rules (the path through the path-exists branch, the bare leaf through the literal grep). Commit: `ci: clear the two standing red gates (PlusRayFibre layer row, stale typst citation)`.

WHAT THIS CHANGES FOR THIS TASK. The measured finding above is no longer reproducible by running the gate; anyone starting here will see a green gate. The underlying defect is untouched: Check 1's dotted-identifier branch still does a literal grep with no namespace resolution, so any future correctly-cited namespaced declaration will fail the same way unless its full dotted name happens to appear verbatim in a docstring. The sibling citation `FormalSystem.Metalogic.Decidability.Compression.decidableValidZTime`, in the same paragraph, resolves ONLY by that accident — the string appears at `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability.lean:181`. That accidental dependency is the clearest remaining evidence and should be the new starting point.

TO REPRODUCE ON DEMAND: reintroduce any fully-qualified declaration name whose dotted form appears in no `.lean` file, or delete the docstring line at `Decidability.lean:181` and re-run the gate.

THE QUESTION THIS TASK STILL OWNS, now sharpened by having three resolution styles live in one paragraph: decide which citation style the manual is entitled to use, and make the checker honour it. Option (a) teach Check 1 real namespace resolution (match a dotted name against `namespace` prefix plus declared leaf); option (b) rule that the manual cites module-path-plus-leaf and document that, making the two surviving fully-qualified declaration citations non-conforming; option (c) keep the literal grep and accept that fully-qualified citations depend on a docstring mentioning them, which is the status quo and is what made this a recurring finding. Option (c) is the one the evidence argues against.

This task is that filing.

THE EDIT TARGET IS THE SOURCE STORE, NOT `.claude/**`. `.claude/` here is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact; a hand-authored file landing there is silently wiped by the next regeneration. Resolved 2026-10-08 from .claude-extensions.json: the source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/<name>/`, and each deployed path mirrors a path under it. Re-resolve before editing rather than trusting this note. Task 736 recorded `.claude/` as STALE for `core filetypes lean typst` at its own dispatch; a 2026-10-08 orchestration re-observed STALE for `core` and `typst`. Remedy is `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh`, never a hand-patch of a deployed file, which would mask the divergence instead of fixing it.

WHAT IS OWED, three items, each independently completable. A partial outcome (two landed, one recorded as declined with a reason) is complete and valid.

(1) `proof_wanted` AS A TRUST SURFACE DISTINCT FROM `sorry`, in the lean extension's metadata-trust-surfaces.md. Source file verified present at `lean/context/project/lean4/domain/metadata-trust-surfaces.md`. The substance, from 736's own Decisions section item 1: a `sorry`-grep returned 0 across all 54 surveyed cslib files while 8 main theorems were unproved, because `proof_wanted` emits NO declaration at all -- so `#print axioms` on such a name returns "unknown constant" rather than a `sorryAx` line, and both the grep and the axiom probe read as clean. 736 logged this as a `false-negative-verification` issue at severity `costly`. The document already catalogues what Lean metadata rows are NOT checked by; this adds the `proof_wanted` row to that catalogue. State the detection method that does work (a `proof_wanted` census per file) alongside the two that do not.

(2) A NEW `external-library-port-assessment.md` PATTERN. Verified absent from the entire source store 2026-10-08; the name implies `lean/context/project/lean4/patterns/`, which exists. The content is 736's own assessment method, which produced a defensible "port nothing" verdict and should be reusable rather than re-derived: toolchain and Mathlib-pin distance as the first gate (76 commits apart was the decisive figure); a `proof_wanted`/`sorry` census of the candidate declarations before treating any as a resource; fork-versus-upstream divergence (the local fork was 155 commits behind, and several files the consuming description listed as upstream were absent from it); the cost of a Lake `require` edge stated as a concrete consequence for the consuming tree rather than as a preference; and the read-only-reference verdict as the default. Record the dissent-and-rejection shape too: 736 reported the dependency alternative the dispatch invited, and rejected it on a stated ground, rather than silently omitting it.

(3) A STEM-COLLISION PASS FOR THE LITERATURE AUDIT. Candidate hosts verified 2026-10-08 under `literature/scripts/`: literature-audit.sh, literature-fidelity-audit.sh, literature-combining-audit.sh; choose by reading them, do not assume. The substance: ids must be matched WHOLE against the global index, never by author-year stem. 736 ran this pass by hand over one sub-index and found THREE unrecorded collisions -- `piterman_2007` (two directories for apparently one paper, divergent fidelity, one `verified_conversion` and one unset); `vardi_1996` (`vardi_1996_automata_ltl` `verified_conversion` versus `vardi_1996_automata-theoretic-ltl` `no_source_pdf`, OPPOSITE fidelity verdicts, and the pre-existing entry carried no hazard); and `vardi_wolper_1986` (file-level versus directory-level records in the same directory, both `verified_conversion`, no fidelity divergence but not interchangeable). The pre-existing `thomas_1997` / `thomas_1997_languages_automata` hazard is the precedent the pass generalizes: one stem, two papers, one of them `no_source_pdf`. A hand pass finding three unrecorded instances in one sub-index is the argument for mechanizing it.

CONSTRAINTS. No Lean, no proofs, no edits under FormalSystem/ or Tests/. Make no claim about cslib's mathematical correctness: the deliverable is agent-system context about how to ASSESS such a library, not a verdict on one. Do not relitigate the "port nothing" recommendation -- it is settled and item (2) documents the method that reached it, not the question. Do not weaken the identity-hazard convention to make a mechanized pass easier; if whole-id matching cannot be mechanized without loosening it, record that as the finding and leave the pass unimplemented. Each of the three items is in a DIFFERENT extension (lean, lean, literature), so expect three separate source-store trees and do not collapse them into one file.

ACCEPTANCE. For each of the three: either the content landed in the named source-store file (new or extended), the deployed tree regenerated via deploy-headless.sh so the deployed copy matches, and a one-line record of what was added; or a recorded reason for declining it, naming what made it not worth doing. Mixed outcomes across the three are expected. The acceptance signal is that the source store, not `.claude/**`, carries every edit -- a change visible only in the deployed tree has not landed.

SCOPE. Source-store files under /home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/{lean,literature}/ plus the regenerated deployed copies. SET file_scope AT PLAN TIME, after reading the three candidate literature-audit scripts to decide which hosts the pass. Do not edit specs/archive/** and do not edit task 736's records: they are the historical source being acted on.

**Description**: Run experiment E4: decide StabSaturated on the Bool fixture's mosaic set, and acquire the Hodkinson-Reynolds Handbook sections

WHY THIS EXISTS. Route R2 -- mosaics and quasimodels -- is the rank-2 route to decidability of L+ over discrete time, behind the seam-gluing route R1 and ahead of the closed and low-ranked alternatives. Its amalgamation precondition is already FREE: `paste` glues two mosaics agreeing at a shared germ, choice-free and with uniqueness, exported as `amalgamate` and `amalgamate_unique`. What gates R2 is a single decidability question that the landed probe fixes as a `Prop` and proves only in a degenerate corner, plus two Handbook sections absent from the corpus. The review named this experiment E4 and left it "open for the next round to attack by name". It was never filed.

THE GATE, precisely. `Probe718Mosaic.StabSaturated` is proved only by `stabSaturated_of_sameState` -- the SAME-STATE corner. R2's hypothesis is that `StabSaturated` is decidable on finite mosaic sets with MIXED germ states, under `[F.IsRegular]`, and that a saturated-set existence theorem holds for L+. Neither is established. Re-verify both declaration names against the tree before relying on them; this description's reading is from 2026-10-05.

WHAT TO DELIVER, in two parts.
(1) The probe: decide `StabSaturated` on the two-state Bool fixture's mosaic set at `box-dot(Fp)`, with MIXED germ states rather than the same-state corner. This is the cheapest test of R2's load-bearing hypothesis.
(2) The sources: the Hodkinson-Reynolds Handbook of Modal Logic sections 5.10 and 5.11 bodies are ABSENT from the corpus, which is why R2 cannot be promoted on textual grounds no matter how the probe comes out. Acquire and ingest them. R2's standing cannot be settled by a probe alone.

THE FALSIFIER, which is a valid outcome. A finite mosaic set, with mixed germ states, on which `StabSaturated` is FALSE yet every finite coherence condition holds. That would close R2 the way filtration was closed, and closing a route on evidence is a complete result -- not a failed task. Record it rather than working around it; the no-weakening rule for wired probes applies.

HARD CONSTRAINTS. No complexity claim. Do not file a fourth certificate class on the strength of anything found here: any certificate class presenting finite fibres is already refuted by `Probe710.not_finite_width_fmp`, and R2 is a quasimodel route, not a certificate class. Do not promote R2 above R1 on probe evidence alone; ranking is the review's, and the Handbook sections are part of what any re-ranking would rest on.

LITERATURE. Part (2) is source acquisition; run with `--lit`.

SCHEDULING. Wiring into `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` collides with the declared file_scope of tasks 720 and 725, and with the two sibling probe tasks filed alongside this one. Run in a separate cycle from each of them.

ACCEPTANCE. One probe file under `specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/` compiling under `lake env lean` and wired into `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh`, whose header states whether `StabSaturated` is decidable on mixed germ states and what that does to R2's standing; plus the two Handbook sections present in the Literature index with an index entry. Either half landing without the other is a [PARTIAL], not a completion.

### 731. Harvest generated surfaces into file scope
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [731_harvest_generated_surfaces_into_file_scope/reports/01_harvest-generated-surfaces.md]
- **Plan**: [731_harvest_generated_surfaces_into_file_scope/plans/01_harvest-generated-surfaces.md]
- **Summary**: [731_harvest_generated_surfaces_into_file_scope/summaries/01_harvest-generated-surfaces-summary.md]

**Description**: Make plan-time file_scope harvest include the generated surfaces that any .lean edit forces, so they stop recurring as undeclared scope extensions.

EVIDENCE: THE SAME OMISSION TWICE, IN CONSECUTIVE TASKS. Two completed tasks independently hit the identical problem. The first recorded the `typst/generated/status.typ` regeneration as an undeclared scope extension after the pre-commit `typst-sync-check.sh --counts-only` gate refused a commit touching a `.lean` file without it. The second, forewarned, named `status.typ` in its own plan's file list — and then hit the *other* generated surface: `check-module-invariants.sh`'s INV check failed because its line additions shifted the generated inventory blocks in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md` and the root `README.md`, neither of which was in its declared file_scope. It root-caused this with disposable `git worktree` bisection, regenerated via `--emit-inventory`, and declared a scope extension. The orchestrator's own postflight independently flagged the same two files: `ADVISORY: task reported modified_files outside its declared file_scope: ["FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md","README.md"]`.

THE PATTERN. These are not planning oversights to be fixed by reminding planners harder. They are mechanically derivable consequences of editing any `.lean` file: the line-count and byte-count tables in the two READMEs, and `typst/generated/status.typ`, are all generated artifacts whose content is a function of the Lean sources. Any plan that touches a `.lean` file will need them, every time, and a plan that omits them produces either a blocked pre-commit gate or a red INV check mid-implementation.

SCOPE. (1) Identify the complete set of generated/derived surfaces that a `.lean` edit forces — at minimum `typst/generated/status.typ` and the inventory blocks in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md` and root `README.md`; check for others by reading the generators (`check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory`, `typst-sync-check.sh`) rather than by enumerating from memory. (2) Decide the mechanism: extend `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` to append these surfaces automatically whenever the harvested scope contains any `.lean` path, or add a plan-template checklist item, or both. Prefer the mechanical option, since the checklist has now been tried once and did not prevent the second occurrence. (3) Ensure the addition is conditional on the repo actually having these generators, so the change does not break sibling repos that lack them. (4) Verify against the two completed tasks' plans: re-running the harvest on them should now produce the surfaces they had to add by hand.

NON-GOALS: no change to the generators themselves or to what they emit; no change to the pre-commit gate's behavior (it is correctly refusing the commit — the plan's declaration is what is incomplete); no retroactive edit to already-completed tasks' file_scope.

### 730. Formal logic implement routing lean capable agent
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Evaluate whether the formal:logic implement-phase routing should resolve to a Lean-capable agent when the plan touches .lean files.

EVIDENCE. The formal extension's manifest declares, explicitly and not as a fallback, `routing_agents.implement["formal:logic"] = "general-implementation-agent"` (confirmed by `command-route-agent.sh implement formal:logic`, which resolves `via=noncore-exact`). The task_type `formal:logic` therefore never reaches `lean-implementation-agent`, which the lean extension reserves for `lean4`, `lean4:lake` and `lean4:version`. A formal:logic task whose work is substantially Lean runs without the lean-lsp MCP path, the Lean-specific implementation contracts, and the lake build guidance that a lean4-typed task receives.

OBSERVED CASE. A completed formal:logic task ran its implement phase under general-implementation-agent across seven files, four of them requiring new Lean theorems in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/PlusWitnessFamily/Incompleteness.lean`, a `#print axioms` baseline probe in `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh`, scoped and full `lake build` runs, and `lake build BimodalTest`. It completed successfully, so this is a potential improvement rather than a defect report — but it did the Lean work without Lean tooling.

FRAME THIS AS AN EVALUATION, NOT A PREDETERMINED CHANGE. `formal:logic` legitimately covers modal-logic research, proof theory and Kripke-semantics work that touches no Lean at all; the research phase already routes it to `logic-research-agent` rather than `lean-research-agent` for exactly that reason. A blanket reroute of the implement phase to `lean-implementation-agent` would therefore be wrong for part of the population. The question to answer is whether routing should become conditional on the plan's harvested `file_scope` containing `.lean` paths, and if so, where that conditional belongs: in the manifest schema (which today maps task_type to agent name with no predicate), in `command-route-agent.sh`, or in the orchestrator's dispatch composition.

SCOPE. (1) Determine how many non-terminal and recently-completed formal / formal:logic / formal:math / formal:physics tasks declare or harvest `.lean` paths in file_scope, to size the affected population rather than generalizing from one case. (2) Decide among: leave as is and document the rationale; reroute formal:logic wholesale; or introduce a file_scope-conditional routing predicate. (3) If a conditional is chosen, specify where it lives and what it does when file_scope is absent (29 non-terminal tasks currently have no file_scope key at all, so the predicate must have a defined behavior for the unknown case, and must not silently pick the heavier agent on no evidence). (4) Apply the resulting decision in the SOURCE STORE (`~/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/formal/manifest.json` or the routing library), never by hand-patching the deployed `.claude/` tree, then redeploy via `deploy-headless.sh`.

NON-GOALS: no change to research-phase or plan-phase routing, both of which are working as intended; no change to the lean extension's own `lean4*` routing; no new agent definitions.

### 729. State json title fields for todo readability
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: repo-hygiene
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Add purpose-written `.title` fields to specs/state.json's active_projects entries so TODO.md stops rendering truncated description prose as task names.

CONTEXT: the entry schema (project_number, project_name, status, task_type, description, topic, dependencies, created, last_updated, file_scope) has no title field populated — all 61 entries lack `.title`. This is NOT a defect. `.title` is an optional field with a documented fallback chain: `generate-todo.sh:188` ("Title fallback: derive from project_name if title is empty", `title="${project_name//_/ }"`) and `generate-task-order.sh:192` ("(.title // .description // .project_name) -- a purpose-written .title is preferred over the top of a long prose .description; .description remains the fallback for title-less tasks, and .project_name is the last resort when both are absent"). The cost of relying on the fallback is readability: TODO.md currently renders the first ~60 characters of `description`, so entries read like "720 — RE-SCOPED 2026-10-03: THIS TASK IS THE CITATION RE-POINT..." and "709 — STANDING (second re-scope; SUPERSEDES BOTH..." — truncated status notes rather than names.

SCOPE: (1) write a concise noun-phrase `.title` for each entry in specs/state.json's active_projects, deriving it from `description` and `project_name`; target under 70 characters, no dates, no status prefixes such as RE-SCOPED/STANDING/HARD/CAPSTONE, no task-number references; prioritize the dependency-ready wave-1 set (127, 128, 178, 257, 298, 464, 481, 502, 559, 566, 570, 604, 616, 617, 664, 709, 711, 712, 713, 714, 716, 720, 722, 723, 725, 726, 728) since those are the entries selected from next. (2) Regenerate TODO.md through the existing generator and confirm the titles render in both the grouped-by-topic listing and the dependency-wave table. (3) Verify the fallback path still works for any entry deliberately left title-less, so the optional-field contract is preserved rather than silently becoming mandatory.

NON-GOALS: no schema change (the field is already supported by both generators); no edit to `description`, `project_name`, `topic`, or any other existing field; no change to the generators themselves; no renaming of task directories, whose names derive from `project_name` and must stay stable.

Effort: small-to-medium, mostly editorial judgment rather than code.

This is not a soft preference: 720's ENTIRE content is re-pointing citations at declaration names that 710 and 706 create, so dispatching 720 first produces either a no-op or a citation to a name that does not exist. The declared dependencies `[706, 710]` already encode this and are UNCHANGED by this revision.

(i) 710 and 720 both declare `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` -- they cannot be batched in one `/orchestrate` cycle; the admission gate defers a batch whose members share a file (`.claude/context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md`). Run that probe task in a different cycle from 720.

WHY THIS MATTERS AT ALL (the surviving half of the original rationale). `FMP/README.md` is a library-facing document that currently tells a reader the two refutations are "compiled outside the build graph", i.e. that the library's own claim about what is refuted rests on files the build does not check. Under Option A that becomes false and misleading rather than merely conservative. The probes also currently live in live-task directories, which is the fragility the evidence collection was built to fix -- a task-directory probe rots and leaves version control on archive, since `specs/archive/` is gitignored -- and the library landing fixes that more strongly than promotion to `specs/evidence/` would have: a theorem in `FormalSystem/` is checked by `lake build` on every commit.

**ENLARGED: THIS IS A CI REPAIR, NOT A CITATION TIDY-UP.** Two of the three `WIRED_REPO` entries in `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` point at files git does not track. Both were tracked under their live task paths (`1b7b8431f`, `b1180dbf8`), were DELETED FROM THE INDEX by the archive commit `70321e938` (349 and 1180 lines), and were then re-pointed to the ignored `specs/archive/` paths by `e8cfcd868`. The gate never checked tracking: `check_probe` tests `[ ! -f "$file" ]` only (`scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh:717-720`), and a grep for `ls-files|check-ignore` over the script finds nothing executable.

**CI CONSEQUENCE.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:119-125` runs `bash scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` after a plain `actions/checkout@v5` (`:22-23`; no workspace cache, only `lean-action`'s `.lake` cache). The step is `continue-on-error: true` (`:121`) but the summary step counts a failed outcome and EXITS 1 (`:352-356`). A checkout that lacks untracked files hits the missing-file branch for both entries, so the gate reports `FAIL (missing: …)` twice and the summary step fails the job. (That last step is an inference from `git ls-files` + `.gitignore:94` + `:717-720` + `:352-356`, each verified; CI logs were not fetched.)

**DELIVERABLE A — CLOSE THE MECHANISM (not optional).** Add a tracked-ness check to `check_probe` beside the `-f` test — `git ls-files --error-unmatch "$file"`, failing loudly — so a `WIRED_REPO` target that git does not track is reported as such. WHY IT RECURS WITHOUT THIS: `/task --abandon` moves a task directory into `specs/archive/` with `mv` (`.claude/context/patterns/task-abandon-mode.md:58-65`) and `/todo` archival does the same (the `70321e938` commit), so ANY `WIRED_REPO` entry pointing into a live task directory is silently un-tracked the day that task is archived, with the gate still green locally for everyone who has the file on disk. Add one paragraph to `check-evidence-probes.sh`'s header stating the rule — TRACKED, NOT MERELY PRESENT — and one sentence beside the `mv` in `task-abandon-mode.md`.

**DELIVERABLE B — RESOLVE THE TWO ENTRIES.** Either force-track them (the precedent is the third `WIRED_REPO` entry, `specs/archive/476_…/evidence/fmp-hypothesis-is-false.lean`, which IS tracked as one of the five force-tracked archive files `ci.yml:239-240` names), or convert them to `WIRED` entries under `specs/evidence/`, or delete them if the landed `Limits/` theorems subsume them. Library prose also cites the force-tracked 476 path (`Limits/FiniteCarrier.lean:55,146`, `BiLasso/Assembly.lean:26`); those citations are NOT broken and are named only so a future force-track audit knows they exist.

**SPENT PASSAGES, to delete rather than carry.** The "PATHS NOT YET BUILT" paragraph is spent: `PlusSlicedCertificate/Limits/` now exists, and the landed library names to cite are `no_finite_carrier_sat`/`not_finite_carrier_fmp` (`Limits/FiniteCarrier.lean:248,:349`) and `no_finite_width_sat`/`not_sliced_complete`/`not_finite_width_fmp` (`Limits/NoFiniteWidth.lean:1200,:1257,:1280`), in namespace `FormalSystem.Metalogic.Decidability.PlusSlicedCertificate.NoFiniteWidth` (`NoFiniteWidth.lean:95-103`) — never the archived `Probe706.`/`Probe710.` spellings. And Deliverable 3 (close as subsumed) NO LONGER APPLIES, per `FMP/README.md:84-98`.

**RUN-ORDER NOTE.** This record is promoted ahead of the sliced-certificate pair: a red CI gate on every push outranks a local defect seen only by whoever runs the 300 s reproducer.

**SCOPE DECISION RECORDED, NOT SILENTLY WIDENED.** `file_scope` gains `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, because Deliverable B's force-track option edits the force-tracked-archive-file list that `ci.yml:239-240` carries. It does NOT gain `.claude/context/patterns/task-abandon-mode.md`: that path is a regenerated deploy artifact, so the real edit target is the source store resolved from `.claude-extensions.json` (`extensions.core.source_dir`, currently outside this repository), which a repository-relative `file_scope` entry cannot express and the overlap gate cannot check. The one-sentence `mv` note of Deliverable A therefore lands in the source store under `context/patterns/task-abandon-mode.md`, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`, and is deliberately outside this record's declared `file_scope`.

### 714. Evaluate full labels in sliced certificate
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Evaluate removing the TailStable junk-position obstruction at its root by carrying full labels in the sliced certificate. Research report 06_tailstable-backward-conjunct-repair.md established that undischargeable snce positions are determined by the certificate position space posAt, not by the witness family: at target bot-S-bot the closure is {bot S bot, bot} with no state shape, so posAt is the same two-label set for every certificate and LocalCoherentLab forces every label of every certifying family to be empty. Because posAt admits every coherent closure-subset agreeing with slab on state shapes only (atom/box/stab) while temporal obligations are pinned by nothing, carrying FULL labels in the certificate rather than only the state part would make posAt a singleton, so no junk position could exist for either obligation direction and arguably neither liveness filter would be needed. This was option 3 in the repair decision; the chosen repair was option 1 (mirror the filter, landing as TailStableMirror/bwdLiveAt). Option 3 was NOT rejected on the merits, only deferred, because it costs the decidable search that FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/PlusSlicedCertificate/Check.lean is built around, i.e. most of Stage 2 again. Determine whether the obstruction can be removed at the root this way, with refactoring permitted as needed to obtain the best result. The central question is whether decidability of the certificate check survives full labels, and if not, whether a weaker label refinement makes posAt small enough to drop both filters while keeping the search decidable. Compare against the landed option-1 design: with full labels, both TailStableMirror and the forward filter would become deletable and the symmetric two-filter shape in Stable.lean would collapse. Also settle whether the superset failure mode (the Fixture.cert pre-period, repaired by absorption rather than by any filter) likewise disappears under full labels or survives independently.

### 713. Formalize ctl star reduction 2exptime lower bound
- **Status**: [HOLD]
- **Held**: 2026-10-03
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: OPTIONAL, FILED NOT SCHEDULED -- AND LOAD-BEARING AS A CHECK. This task stays unscheduled: status HOLD (user ruling 2026-10-03; prior_status not_started), dependencies [], nothing depends on it. What has changed since it was filed is the claim that "none of the L-plus programme's recommendations rest on it." That is no longer accurate. Its 2EXPTIME lower bound -- ARGUED, not formalized -- is the sanity ceiling that three live records cite against any proposed decision procedure for L-plus. It is load-bearing as a check even while unproved.

ITS ROLE: THE SANITY CEILING. Three records USE this task's argued lower bound as the bound-sanity check -- nothing cheaper than 2EXPTIME is a credible decision procedure. All three were re-verified against the tree on 2026-10-07 (task 738), at their archive locations:

1. specs/archive/718_omega_sequence_decidability_full_lplus/reports/02_ranked-route-analysis.md (archived), the "Complexity sanity check" bullet at line 258: "the CTL* 2EXPTIME-hardness reduction (filed, argued in both directions) is the lower bound; determinization-based upper bounds are doubly exponential, which is consistent. Any proposal cheaper than 2EXPTIME is prima facie wrong. No bound committed."

2. Task 719's description (ray layer, seam gluing and stab fibre; completed and archived at specs/archive/719_ray_layer_seam_gluing_and_stab_fibre, record in specs/archive/state.json -- now provenance rather than a live document), Deliverable 5: "DO NOT LAND A COMPLEXITY CLAIM; the CTL-star 2EXPTIME lower bound is the sanity check on any proposed bound."

3. specs/archive/721_decidability_programme_review_l_and_lplus/reports/01_decidability-programme-review.md (archived) section 3.1 (L-plus routes forward), line 147 -- its Section C ruling on this task is at line 183: "the CTL* 2EXPTIME-hardness argument (task 713, ARGUED) is the lower bound on any proposed procedure; nothing cheaper is credible; no bound is committed here."

ARGUED, NOT FORMALIZED -- AND EVERY CITING RECORD MUST SAY SO. The bound is an argument in prose, not a machine-checked theorem. No Lean declaration establishes it; there is no CTL-star syntax or semantics in the tree to state it against. Every record that cites it must describe it that way, with the ARGUED qualifier attached, exactly as the three records above do. A citation that presents the bound as established, or that drops the qualifier, is a defect in that record.

NO UPPER-BOUND CLAIM MAY LAND FROM THIS TASK. The programme commits to no complexity bound. This task's content is a LOWER bound used as a ceiling: it rules proposals out, it never certifies that any procedure achieves a cost. Deriving or landing an upper-bound claim from it -- in this task's artifacts, in a citing record, or in the write-up -- is a defect, not a result. The asymmetry is the point: a lower bound says "nothing cheaper is credible", it does not say "this much suffices."

GOAL. Raise the 2EXPTIME-hardness of L-plus Z-time validity from ARGUED to MACHINE-CHECKED. The argument is given in both directions in specs/archive/706_lplus_finite_model_property_and_completeness/reports/01_lplus-finite-model-property-research.md section Q4 ("The true order of the state bound"), completing what task 703's round-2 report argued in one direction only -- now archived at specs/archive/703_lplus_compression_and_completeness/reports/02_semantics-first-compression-research.md section 1.6 ("The complexity picture").

THE REDUCTION. Translate CTL-star by A |-> stab and X |-> bottom U ., with non-strict U rendered as e or (g and (g U e)). FORWARD: a rooted total Kripke structure becomes bi-serial by adding one fresh state with a self-loop and an edge to the root, which leaves forward paths from the original states unchanged; stab at (sigma, t) with sigma t = w ranges over histories through w, whose futures are exactly the forward paths from w, and the translation is pure-future so its truth depends only on the future (truth_congr_agreeFrom). BACKWARD: from a Z-model of the translation at (sigma, t), the Kripke structure of the state graph forward-reachable from sigma t satisfies the CTL-star formula at sigma t, by the same two facts read backwards. CTL-star satisfiability is over arbitrary, possibly infinite structures, so no finiteness is needed anywhere.

WHAT IT WOULD BUY. Formalization would turn the ceiling from an argued check into a theorem-backed one: the sharpest available limit on how good ANY certificate class can be. As task 706's Q4 and the archived 703 round-2 section 1.6 record the consequence, with the reduction a theorem no complete certificate class with singly exponential, polynomially checkable certificates could exist for full L-plus without a complexity-theoretic surprise.

CANONICAL WORDING OF THE CONSEQUENCE (quote this, verbatim, in any record that repeats it). L-plus Z-time validity -- equivalently, by negation, L-plus Z-time satisfiability -- is 2EXPTIME-hard by the CTL-star reduction. ARGUED, NOT FORMALIZED: no Lean declaration establishes it. CONSEQUENCE, stated for NON-VALIDITY: if every non-valid formula had a certificate of size singly exponential in the closure size, checkable in time polynomial in the certificate's size, then non-validity (satisfiability of the negation) would be in NEXPTIME and, equivalently, validity would be in co-NEXPTIME; hence 2EXPTIME would be contained in NEXPTIME, equivalently in co-NEXPTIME, since 2EXPTIME is closed under complement. That containment is not refuted but is widely disbelieved. The two class names are the same statement read from opposite sides of the complement: a record may name either, but must say which problem it is placing (NON-VALIDITY with NEXPTIME; VALIDITY with co-NEXPTIME). This is a LOWER bound used as a ceiling: nothing cheaper than 2EXPTIME is credible. It says nothing about any cost sufficing for L-plus and must never be restated as an upper bound. Sources: 706 Q4 (validity / co-NEXPTIME reading) and 703 round-2 section 1.6 (satisfiability / NEXPTIME reading), reconciled 2026-10-07 (task 738): the apparent divergence was one of presentation, not content.

WHAT IT COSTS. A CTL-star syntax and semantics must be added to the tree, which is substantial standalone work the programme otherwise has no need for. Task 706 rates the risk that the reduction has a gap as LOW (its R4) and records it as affecting the complexity picture only.

KEEP-OR-ABANDON IS A PHASE 0 USER RULING, NOT MADE HERE. This revision records the task's role; it does not decide the task's fate and does not schedule it. The decidability-programme review's Section C (specs/archive/721_decidability_programme_review_l_and_lplus/followup-scope-spec.md, Section C at line 220) restates the choice as the user's: keep this as a write-up note, or abandon it. RECOMMENDATION ONLY, not a decision: keep it as a write-up note, because it is load-bearing as a check even while unproved -- abandoning the record would leave the three citing documents pointing at nothing. If the ruling is to abandon, the three citing records need their sanity-check bullets re-sourced to the argument's actual homes (706's Q4 and the archived 703 round-2 section 1.6) rather than to this task.

CONSEQUENCES: (i) the 'STATUS OF THE STATEMENT ... OPEN, NOT REFUTED' paragraph below is superseded and must not be read as current; (ii) decidability of L-plus Z-time validity does NOT follow by this route and the route is closed, not merely blocked; (iii) the omega-automata determinization blocker was the blocker for PROVING the statement, so it no longer gates this record -- whether that blocker task is still wanted for other purposes is a separate question and is NOT settled here; (iv) a successor class must present INFINITE fibres (root paths of a finite class graph), with no checker precedent, and is to be filed as research-first only if decidability of full L-plus remains a programme goal. Original description follows, superseded as noted.

THE STATEMENT, to be held open and unamended until proved: every Z-time non-validity of a PlusFormula admits a TAIL-STABLE SLICED certificate -- the class task 703's amended Stage 2 lands -- meeting its Certifies predicate. Together with the soundness theorem and the decidable checker that Stage 2 also lands, this would yield decidability of L-plus Z-time validity, which is the result the L-plus completeness programme exists to reach.

STATUS OF THE STATEMENT, per specs/archive/706_lplus_finite_model_property_and_completeness/reports/01_lplus-finite-model-property-research.md decision D3: it is OPEN, NOT REFUTED. What task 706 refuted is the FINITE-CARRIER statement only (Probe706.not_finite_carrier_fmp, sorry-free, machine-checked), which killed the finite-graph certificate type. The sliced statement has no counterexample and none is expected; task 710 exists to attack it adversarially before task 709 spends 60-100 hours downstream of it.

WHY BLOCKED. The only known proof route needs omega-automata determinization, which neither this tree nor Mathlib has (task 711, filed as the named blocker). The alternative -- a direct construction over the product of the state graph with the Hintikka types -- is refuted by the classical new-path example Reynolds 2001 records: in the limit, a step-by-step or filtration construction produces many more paths than were ever chosen explicitly, and a new path can postpone an eventuality forever.

NO BOUND IS COMMITTED and none should be. Doubly exponential slice width is the honest expectation for full L-plus: L-plus Z-time validity is 2EXPTIME-hard by the CTL-star reduction argued in both directions in that report's section Q4, so a singly exponential certificate checkable in time polynomial in its size would place a 2EXPTIME-hard problem in co-NEXPTIME. No order is claimed for the tail periods either -- the natural tail-stability argument gives a third exponential, but that is an artefact of that argument, and a type-recurrence cut on slices may do better as the L side does for a single history.

DO NOT UNBLOCK THIS TASK ON AN ARGUMENT. Unblock it only on a proof, or convert it to a refutation task if task 710 produces a counterexample.
